<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\label{def:mixing_matrix}
	Given an undirected connected graph $\mathcal{G} = (\mathcal{V}, \mathcal{E})$, where $\mathcal{V} = \{1, 2, \cdots, n\}$ is the set of vertices and $\mathcal{E}$ is the set of edges.
	A matrix $\mathbf{W} = (W_{ij}) \in \mathbb{R}^{n \times n}$ is called a mixing matrix if it satisfies the following four conditions:
	\begin{compactenum}[(1)]
		\item $W_{ij} = 0$ for every $i \ne j$ with $(i, j) \notin \mathcal{E}$.
		\item $\mathbf{W}$ is symmetric.
		\item $\operatorname{Null}\big((\mathbb{I}_n -\mathbf{W})\otimes \mathbb{I}_p\big) = \mathcal D$, where $\mathbb{I}_n$ is the $n \times n$ identity matrix.
		\item $\mathbb{I}_n \succeq \mathbf{W} \succ -\mathbb{I}_n$.
	\end{compactenum}
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
We recall here some standard constructions of mixing matrices satisfying Definition~\ref{def:mixing_matrix}.
	These matrices have been widely used in the literature, including \cite{chen2012fast,Jakovetic2011,mateos2015distributed,mateos2017distributed,Nedic2009,shi2015extra,shi2015proximal}.
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] Symmetric doubly stochastic matrix: 
		Let $\mathbf{W} = \mathbf{W}^\top$ be a nonnegative doubly stochastic
		matrix compatible with $\mathcal{G}$, i.e.,
		$W_{ij}=0$ whenever $i\neq j$ and $(i,j) \notin \mathcal{E}$.
		If its support graph is connected and $W_{ii} > 0$ for all $i\in[n]$, then $\mathbf{W}$ satisfies Definition~\ref{def:mixing_matrix}.
		
		\item[(ii)] Laplacian-based matrix: $\mathbf{W} = \mathbb{I} - \frac{\mathcal{L}}{\gamma}$, where $\mathcal{L}$ is the Laplacian matrix of the graph $\mathcal{G}$ and $\gamma > \frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathcal{L})$ is a scaling parameter.
		Let $\mathcal{V}_i := \{j \in \mathcal{V} : (i, j) \in \mathcal{E}\}$ be the neighbor set of node $i$ and $d_i = \vert \mathcal{V}_i \vert$ denote the degree of node $i$.
		When $\lambda_{\max}(\mathcal{L})$ is unavailable, $\gamma = \max_{i \in \mathcal{V}}\{d_i\} + \epsilon$ for some small $\epsilon > 0$ (e.g., $\epsilon = 1$) can be used.
		
		\item[(iii)] Metropolis constant edge weight matrix:
		\begin{equation*}
			\arraycolsep=0.2em
			W_{ij} = \left\{ 
			\begin{array}{ll}
				\frac{1}{\epsilon + \max\{d_i, d_j\}} & \text{if } (i,j) \in \mathcal{E} \ \text{and} \ i \neq j \vspace{1ex}\\
				1 - \sum_{k \in \mathcal{N}_i} \frac{1}{\epsilon + \max\{d_i, d_k\}} \quad & \text{if } i = j, \vspace{1ex}\\
				0 & \text{otherwise},
			\end{array}
			\right.
		\end{equation*}
		for some small $\epsilon > 0$ (e.g., $\epsilon = 1$), where $\mathcal{V}_i$ and $d_i$ are defined in (ii).
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- assumption-1: body_md -->
\label{as:restricted_dual_gap}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, there exists an interior solution $u^{\dagger}$ of \eqref{eq:DGE}, i.e., $u^{\dagger} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi) \cap \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T_i))$.
<!-- end assumption-1 -->
<!-- assumption-2: body_md -->
\label{as:boundary_regularity}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, the following conditions hold:
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] $\bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{dom}(T_i)$ is bounded and $\bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathrm{dom}(T_i)\right) \ne \emptyset$.

	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] There exist nonempty closed convex sets $\mathcal{C}_i \subseteq \mathbb{R}^p$ and a mapping $S_i: \mathcal{C}_i \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^p$ with $\mathrm{dom}(S_i) = \mathcal{C}_i$, $i = 1, \cdots, n$, such that $T_i = S_i + \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}$.
	Moreover, $S_i$ satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:S_bounded}
		M_S := \max_{i=1, \cdots, n} \sup\Big\{ \Verts_i\Vert : u \in \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathcal{C}_i, \ s_i \in S_iu \Big\} < +\infty.
	\end{equation}
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end assumption-2 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{le:gap_function}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that $\Phi_i := G_i + T_i$ is maximally monotone.
	Furthermore, suppose that the set $\mathcal{B}$ is chosen as follows:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, let $\mathcal{B}$ be  a nonempty compact convex set satisfying $u^{\dagger} \in \mathrm{int}(\mathcal{B})$ and $\mathcal{B} \subset \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T_i))$.
		
		\item Under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}$\mathrm{(i)}$, let $\mathcal{B} := \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{dom}(T_i)$.
	\end{compactitem}
	Then, 	$\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\cdot)$ defined by \eqref{eq:res_gap_func} has the following properties:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}$ is well-defined on $\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)$. 
		Moreover, $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(u) \geq 0$ for all $u \in \mathrm{dom}(\Phi)$;
		\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$]
		If $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$, then $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(u^{\star}) = 0$.
		\item[$\mathrm{(iii)}$]
		If $u^{\star} \in \mathcal{B}$ and $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(u^{\star}) = 0$, then $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- assumption-3: body_md -->
\label{as:FKM_assumption}
	The composite inclusion \eqref{eq:GE} satisfies the following conditions:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] (\textbf{\textit{Maximal monotonicity}}) $G + T$ is maximally monotone.
		\item[(ii)] (\textbf{\textit{Lipschitz continuity}}) $G$ is $L$-Lipschitz continuous.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end assumption-3 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{le:resolvent_properties}
	Let $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{L})$.
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:FKM_assumption}, the resolvent $J_{\lambda T} := (\mathbb{I} + \lambda T)^{-1}$ is well-defined, single-valued, and $\frac{1}{1-\lambda L}$-Lipschitz continuous.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
Since $G$ is $L$-Lipschitz continuous, using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can prove that
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}	
			\langle (L\mathbb{I} - G)u - (L\mathbb{I} - G)u', u - u'\rangle &=& L\Vertu - u'\Vert^2 - \langle Gu - Gu', u - u'\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\geq& L\Vertu - u'\Vert^2 - \VertGu - Gu'\Vert\Vertu - u'\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\geq& 0, \quad \forall u, u' \in \mathbb{R}^p.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Hence, $L\mathbb{I} - G$ is monotone and continuous, and has full domain.
	Moreover, since $\Phi = G + T$ is maximally monotone, it also follows that $T + L\mathbb{I} = \Phi + (L\mathbb{I} - G)$ is maximally monotone.
	Then, since $0 < \lambda < \frac{1}{L}$, it is easy to show that the operator $\mathbb{I} + \lambda T = (1 - \lambda L)\mathbb{I} + \lambda(T + L\mathbb{I})$ is maximally $(1-\lambda L)$-strongly monotone. Therefore, the resolvent $J_{\lambda T} = (\mathbb{I} + \lambda T)^{-1}$ is single-valued on $\mathbb{R}^p$ and $\frac{1}{1 - \lambda L}$-Lipschitz continuous.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{le:FKM_key_est1}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM_assumption} holds for \eqref{eq:GE} and $\mathrm{zer}(\Phi)\neq\emptyset$.
	For given $r > 0$, $\eta > 0$, and $\nu > 0$, let the parameters in \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme} be updated by
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est1_params}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		t_{k+1} = t_k + 1,  \quad \beta_k := \frac{c_1 \eta(t_k - r)}{t_k}, \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}, \quad \text{and} \quad \gamma_k := 1,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	for some $c_1 > 0$ and $t_0 > \max\{1, r, \frac{\nu}{\eta}\}$.
	                                                                          
	Let us define the following \textbf{Lyapunov function}:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_Lyapunov_func}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{P}_k &:=& \frac{a_k}{2}\Vertw^k\Vert^2 + r(t_k - r) \langle w^k, x^k - z^k\rangle + \frac{r^2(r-1)}{2\nu_k}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 +  \frac{d_k}{2} \Verte^k\Vert^2 ,
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	then we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est1}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{P}_k - \mathcal{P}_{k+1} &\geq& \frac{\hat{a}_{k+1} - a_{k+1}}{2} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + r(r-1)\langle w^{k+1}, x^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle + \frac{b_k}{2} \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2  \vspace{1ex}\\ 
			&& + {~} \frac{\phi_k}{2} \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 + \frac{\hat{d}_{k+1} - d_{k+1}}{2}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2,
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$,  $M^2 := 2(1+\omega)L^2$ for some $\omega \geq 0$, and 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est1_coeffs}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		a_k &:=& \eta t_k (t_k - r) \big[ 1 - \frac{c_1 (t_k - r)}{t_k} \big], \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{a}_{k+1} &:=& t_k [(1-c_1)\eta t_k - \nu_k], \vspace{1ex}\\
		b_k &:=& t_k (r \eta - \nu_k) + \frac{(r-1)\nu (t_k-1)}{t_k}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\phi_k &:=& t_k[\eta (t_k - r) - M^2 \eta^2 (\eta t_k - \nu_k)], \vspace{1ex}\\
		d_k &:=& t_k \big[ \frac{\beta_k^2 t_k}{c_1 \eta} + M^2 \eta^2 (\eta t_k - \nu_k) \big], \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{d}_{k+1} &:=& t_k \big[\omega (\eta t_k - \nu_k) - (r-1)\nu (t_k-1)\big].
	\end{array}
	\right.
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{le:FKM_key_est2}
	Under the same settings as in Lemma~\ref{le:FKM_key_est1}, suppose that $t_0$ satisfies \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0} and other parameters are chosen such that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est2_params}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\hspace{-1ex}
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		r > 2, \quad \delta \in (0, r - 2), \quad \omega = r^2 - 3r + 3, \quad 0 < \eta \leq \frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+\omega}L}, \ \text{and} \ 0 < \nu < (r - 2 - \delta)\eta.
	\end{array}
	\hspace{-1ex}
	\end{equation}
	Then, $\mathcal{P}_k$ defined in \eqref{eq:FKM_Lyapunov_func} satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k - \mathcal{P}_{k+1} &\geq& \frac{[(r-2-\delta)\eta - \nu]t_{k+1}}{2} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\eta t_{k+1}}{2} \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&&  + {~} \frac{\eta t_k^2}{8} \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 + \frac{\eta t_{k+1}^2}{8}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{le:FKM_key_est3}
	Under the same settings as in Lemmas~\ref{le:FKM_key_est1} and \ref{le:FKM_key_est2}, $\mathcal{P}_k$ given in \eqref{eq:FKM_Lyapunov_func} satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_key_est3}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k &\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2}{2\nu}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{th:FKM1_convergence}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM_assumption} holds for \eqref{eq:GE}, $\mathrm{zer}(\Phi) \neq\emptyset$, and $t_0$ is given by \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0}. 
	Let $\{(\hat{x}^k, x^k, y^k, z^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme} where the parameters are chosen as
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 2, \quad \delta \in (0, r-2), \quad \omega := r^2 - 3r + 3, \quad 0 < \eta \leq \frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+\omega}L}, \quad 0 < \nu <  (r - 2 - \delta)\eta,
			\vspace{1ex}\\
			t_{k} := k + t_0, 
			\quad \beta_k := \frac{\delta \eta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_k}, \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}, \quad \text{and} \quad \gamma_k := 1.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold. 
	\begin{itemize}
	\itemsep=-0.3em
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$]~\textbf{$($The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates$)$} 
	There exists $\xi^k \in Tx^k$ such that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcllcl}		
		\VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \dfrac{r-1}{\delta \eta(k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{R}_0^2 \ \ \text{and} \ \
		\VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \dfrac{4(r-1)}{\delta\eta(k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{R}_0^2,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $\mathcal{R}_0^2 := \frac{\eta (t_0 - r) [(2(r-1) - \delta)t_0 + r\delta]}{2(r-1)}\Vertw^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)t_0}{\nu (t_0 - 1)}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2$.
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$]~\textbf{$($The $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates$)$} 
	There exist $\xi^k \in Tx^k$ for all $k \geq 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}		
		\lim_{k \to \infty} k^2 \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = 0 \qquad \text{and} \qquad 
		\lim_{k \to \infty} k^2 \VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 = 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	\item[$\mathrm{(iii)}$]~\textbf{$($The convergence of iterates$)$} 
	All $\{x^k\}$, $\{\hat{x}^k\}$, $\{y^k\}$, and $\{z^k\}$ converge to $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
For readability, we divide the proof of this theorem into the following steps.
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent\textbf{Step 1. (\textit{The summability bounds}).}
	Summing up \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2} from $k := 0$ to $k := K$ and noting that $\mathcal{P}_k \geq 0$ for all $k \geq 0$ (see \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3}), taking the limit of the result as $K \to \infty$, we can show that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}		
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty  (k + t_0) \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2}{(r-2-\delta)\eta - \nu}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0) \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2}{\eta}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty (k + t_0)^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{8}{\eta}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0)^2 \VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{8}{\eta} \mathcal{P}_0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Since $y^{-1} = z^0 = x^0$, utilizing $\mathcal{R}_0$ from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, we can prove that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}		
		\mathcal{P}_0 &=& \frac{a_0}{2}\Vertw^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)}{2\nu_0}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		
		&=& \frac{\eta t_0 (t_0 - r)}{2} \left[ 1 - \frac{\delta (t_0 - r)}{2(r-1)t_0}\right]\Vertw^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)t_0}{2\nu (t_0 - 1)}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		
		& = & \frac{\eta (t_0 - r) [(2(r-1) - \delta)t_0 + r\delta]}{4(r-1)}\Vertw^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)t_0}{2\nu (t_0 - 1)}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2 = \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2}.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Substituting this bound into \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof1}, we obtain 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_summability_bounds}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lclcl}		
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty  (k + t_0) \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{1}{(r-2-\delta)\eta - \nu} \mathcal{R}_0^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0) \VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{1}{\eta}\mathcal{R}_0^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0)^2 \VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{4}{\eta} \mathcal{R}_0^2 &<& +\infty,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent\textbf{Step 2. (\textit{The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}}).}
	Now, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2} of Lemma~\ref{le:FKM_key_est2}, we can use induction to show that $\mathcal{P}_k \leq \mathcal{P}_0 \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2}$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	Moreover, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k &\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2}{2\nu}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 \geq \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining these facts, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2(r-1)}{\delta \eta (k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{r-1}{\delta \eta(k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{R}_0^2,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	which is the first bound of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, where $\xi^k \in Tx^k$.
	
	Next, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est1_coeffs}, using $\beta_k$ from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_params} and $\eta t_k - \nu_k \geq 0$, we achieve
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		d_k &=&  t_k \left[ \frac{\beta_k^2 t_k}{c_1\eta} + M^2 \eta^2 \left(\eta t_k - \nu_k\right) \right] 
		= \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 + M^2\eta^2 t_k (\eta t_k - \nu_k) 
		\geq \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Then, utilizing \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3} and Young's inequality as $ 2\Vertw^k\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2 \geq \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k &\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{4(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{\delta \eta}{4(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{8(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2\Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this inequality and $\mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2}$, we obtain	
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{8(r-1)}{\delta \eta (k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{P}_k = \frac{4(r-1)}{\delta\eta(k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{R}_0^2,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	which is the second bound of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, where $\xi^k \in Tx^k$.
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent\textbf{Step 3. (\textit{Some intermediate results}).}
	Rearranging \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2}, noting that $e^k = \hat{w}^k - w^k$, and then applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1}, we can prove that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \mathcal{P}_k$ exists, and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	By Young's inequality, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertw^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2\Vertw^{k+1} - \hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Utilizing these relations, we can easily derive from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2} that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof3}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcllcl}
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vertw^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty
			\qquad \text{and} \qquad 
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent\textbf{Step 4. (\textit{The $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates}).}
	Let us denote $v^k := r(z^k - x^k)$.
	Then, from the first and the second lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we can derive that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof4}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^k = t_k (\hat{x}^k - x^k) = t_k(y^k - x^k) + t_k (\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Using this expression and $x^{k+1} - y^k = \eta (\hat{w}^k - \hat{w}^{k+1})$ from the second and the third lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we can derive
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^{k+1} - \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} v^k &=& r(z^{k+1} - z^k) - r(x^{k+1} - y^k) + r(\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& -\nu_k \hat{w}^{k+1} - r\eta(\hat{w}^k - \hat{w}^{k+1})  + r(\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& (r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	This is equivalent to $v^{k+1} = \left(1 - \frac{r}{t_k}\right)v^k + \frac{r}{t_k} \cdot \frac{t_k}{r}\left[ (r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k \right]$.
	Since $\frac{r}{t_k} \in \left(0, 1\right)$, from the convexity of $\Vert\cdot\Vert^2$, Young's inequality, $\nu_k = \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k} \leq \nu$, and $\beta_k = \frac{\delta \eta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_k} \leq \frac{\eta}{2}$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertv^{k+1}\Vert^2 &\leq&  \left(1 - \frac{r}{t_k}\right) \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{t_k}{r} \Vert(r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex} \\
		&\leq&  \Vertv^k\Vert^2 - \frac{r}{t_k} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{2t_k(r\eta - \nu_k)^2}{r}\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + 2r\beta_k^2 t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vertv^k\Vert^2 - \frac{r}{t_k} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{4(r^2\eta^2 + \nu^2)t_k}{r}\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{r\eta^2}{4} t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1} to the last inequality and using the second bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_summability_bounds}, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 = r^2 \lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 \text{ exists \quad and \quad } \sum\limits_{k=0}^\infty \frac{r}{t_k}\Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 < \infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Utilizing Lemma~\ref{le:A2}, these relations imply
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 = 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Next, using $x^{k+1} - y^k = -\eta (\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k)$ from \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2} and the second bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof3}, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof6}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2\Vertx^{k+1} - y^k\Vert^2 = \eta^2 \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Using again \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof4}, by Young's inequality and $\beta_k \leq \frac{\eta}{2}$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2r^2}{t_k}\Vertz^k - x^k\Vert^2 + 2t_k(\eta - \beta_k)^2\Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \leq \frac{2r^2}{t_k}\Vertz^k - x^k\Vert^2 + 2\eta^2 t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2, \vspace{1ex}\\
		t_k \Vertx^{k+1} - x^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2t_k\Vertx^{k+1} - y^k\Vert^2 + 2t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using the second summability bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}, \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}, and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof6}, we can derive from the last inequalities that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof7}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2 < +\infty
		\text{\quad and \quad}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vertx^{k+1} - x^k\Vert^2 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Now, since $\mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2} < +\infty$, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3} of Lemma~\ref{le:FKM_key_est3}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k-r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2}{2\nu}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2} < +\infty,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	which implies that $\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \leq M := \frac{\nu \mathcal{R}_0^2}{r^2}$.
	Using this bound, Young's inequality, and $\nu_k \leq \nu$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.1em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		-\frac{2\nu_k}{r} \langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle &\leq& \frac{\nu_k t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu_k}{rt_k^2}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \leq \frac{\nu t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{M\nu}{rt_k^2}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		-\frac{2\nu_k}{r} \langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\rangle &\leq& \frac{\nu_k t_k}{r} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu_k}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2 \leq \frac{\nu t_k}{r} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using the last line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, $\nu_k \leq \nu$, and the last two relations, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{\star}\Vert^2 &=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + 2\langle z^{k+1} - z^k, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \Vertz^{k+1} - z^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1} + e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{\nu_k^2}{r^2}\Vertw^{k+1} + e^{k+1}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, x^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\rangle\vspace{1ex}\\
		&& - {~} \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle  \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq&  \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \left(\frac{2\nu}{r} + \frac{\nu t_k}{r}\right) \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{M\nu}{rt_k^2}.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since the last four terms on the right-hand side are summable due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}, applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1}, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2$ exists.
	However, since $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 = 0$ due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1}, using the triangle inequality as $|\Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert - \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert| \leq \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert$, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2$ also exists.
	
	From the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, we have $\{(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2\}$ is bounded, i.e., there exists $\bar{M} > 0$ such that $(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 \leq \bar{M}^2$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	Using this inequality and $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 = 0$  from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1}, we can show that as $k \to \infty$,
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		(t_k - r)^2[\langle w^k, x^k - z^k\rangle]^2 \leq (t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 \leq \bar{M}^2\Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 \to 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	This inequality implies that $\lim_{k \to \infty} (t_k - r)\langle w^k, x^k - z^k\rangle = 0$.
	Moreover, from the last line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}, we also have $\lim_{k \to \infty} t_k^2 \Verte^k\Vert^2 = 0$.
	
	Now, from \eqref{eq:FKM_Lyapunov_func}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k &:=& \frac{a_k}{2}\Vertw^k\Vert^2 + r(t_k - r) \langle w^k, x^k - z^k\rangle + \frac{r^2(r-1)}{2\nu_k}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 +  \frac{d_k}{2} \Verte^k\Vert^2 ,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\lim_{k \to \infty} \mathcal{P}_k$ and $\lim_{k \to \infty}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2$ exist, and $\lim_{k \to \infty} (t_k - r)\langle w^k, x^k - z^k\rangle = 0$ and $\lim_{k \to \infty} d_k \Verte^k\Vert^2 = 0$ due to $d_k = \mathcal{O}\big(t_k^2\big)$, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} a_k \Vertw^k\Vert^2$ exists.
	By the definition of $a_k$, one can easily show that $\frac{t_k^2}{a_k} \to \frac{1}{\eta(1-c_1)}$ as $k \to \infty$.
	Therefore, the existence of $\lim_{k \to \infty} a_k \Vertw^k\Vert^2$ implies the existence of $\lim_{k \to \infty} t_k^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2$.
	However, since $\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vertw^k\Vert^2 < +\infty$ by the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}, by Lemma~\ref{le:A2}, we can argue that $\lim_{k \to \infty} t_k^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 = 0$, which proves the first limit of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates}.
	
	Using Young's inequality as $\Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \leq 2\Vertw^k\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2$, the first limit of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates}, and the last line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}, we obtain the second limit of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates}.
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent\textbf{Step 5. (\textit{The convergence of the iterates}).}
	We have proved in \textbf{Step 4} that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert$ exists for every $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$. 
	Moreover, from the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof1}, we also have $\lim_{k \to \infty} \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert = 0$ for some $\xi^k \in Tx^k$, which means that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertw^k\Vert = 0$ for $(x^k, w^k) \in \mathrm{gra}(\Phi)$.
	Applying Opial's lemma, we conclude that $\{x^k\}$ converges  to a solution $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	Next, since $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert = 0$ and $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - y^k\Vert = 0$ due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof7}, we conclude that $\{y^k\}$ and $\{z^k\}$ also converge  to a solution $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	Finally, from the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, we can also show that $\{\hat{x}^k\}$ converges to $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- head-1: body_md -->
Summing up \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2} from $k := 0$ to $k := K$ and noting that $\mathcal{P}_k \geq 0$ for all $k \geq 0$ (see \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3}), taking the limit of the result as $K \to \infty$, we can show that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}		
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty  (k + t_0) \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2}{(r-2-\delta)\eta - \nu}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0) \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2}{\eta}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty (k + t_0)^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{8}{\eta}\mathcal{P}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0)^2 \VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{8}{\eta} \mathcal{P}_0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Since $y^{-1} = z^0 = x^0$, utilizing $\mathcal{R}_0$ from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, we can prove that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}		
		\mathcal{P}_0 &=& \frac{a_0}{2}\Vertw^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)}{2\nu_0}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
<!-- end head-1 -->
<!-- head-2: body_md -->
Now, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2} of Lemma~\ref{le:FKM_key_est2}, we can use induction to show that $\mathcal{P}_k \leq \mathcal{P}_0 \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2}$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	Moreover, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{P}_k &\geq& \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2}{2\nu}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 \geq \frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k - r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining these facts, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2(r-1)}{\delta \eta (k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{r-1}{\delta \eta(k + t_0 - r)^2}\mathcal{R}_0^2,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	which is the first bound of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates}, where $\xi^k \in Tx^k$.
<!-- end head-2 -->
<!-- head-3: body_md -->
Rearranging \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2}, noting that $e^k = \hat{w}^k - w^k$, and then applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1}, we can prove that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \mathcal{P}_k$ exists, and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vertw^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	By Young's inequality, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertw^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2\Vertw^{k+1} - \hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 + 2\Vert\hat{w}^k - w^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Utilizing these relations, we can easily derive from \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2} that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof3}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcllcl}
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vertw^{k+1} - w^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty
			\qquad \text{and} \qquad 
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 &<& +\infty.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end head-3 -->
<!-- head-4: body_md -->
Let us denote $v^k := r(z^k - x^k)$.
	Then, from the first and the second lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we can derive that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof4}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^k = t_k (\hat{x}^k - x^k) = t_k(y^k - x^k) + t_k (\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Using this expression and $x^{k+1} - y^k = \eta (\hat{w}^k - \hat{w}^{k+1})$ from the second and the third lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we can derive
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^{k+1} - \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} v^k &=& r(z^{k+1} - z^k) - r(x^{k+1} - y^k) + r(\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& -\nu_k \hat{w}^{k+1} - r\eta(\hat{w}^k - \hat{w}^{k+1})  + r(\eta - \beta_k)\hat{w}^k \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& (r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	This is equivalent to $v^{k+1} = \left(1 - \frac{r}{t_k}\right)v^k + \frac{r}{t_k} \cdot \frac{t_k}{r}\left[ (r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k \right]$.
	Since $\frac{r}{t_k} \in \left(0, 1\right)$, from the convexity of $\Vert\cdot\Vert^2$, Young's inequality, $\nu_k = \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k} \leq \nu$, and $\beta_k = \frac{\delta \eta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_k} \leq \frac{\eta}{2}$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertv^{k+1}\Vert^2 &\leq&  \left(1 - \frac{r}{t_k}\right) \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{t_k}{r} \Vert(r\eta - \nu_k)\hat{w}^{k+1} - r\beta_k \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex} \\
		&\leq&  \Vertv^k\Vert^2 - \frac{r}{t_k} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{2t_k(r\eta - \nu_k)^2}{r}\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + 2r\beta_k^2 t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vertv^k\Vert^2 - \frac{r}{t_k} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 + \frac{4(r^2\eta^2 + \nu^2)t_k}{r}\Vert\hat{w}^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{r\eta^2}{4} t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1} to the last inequality and using the second bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_summability_bounds}, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertv^k\Vert^2 = r^2 \lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 \text{ exists \quad and \quad } \sum\limits_{k=0}^\infty \frac{r}{t_k}\Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 < \infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Utilizing Lemma~\ref{le:A2}, these relations imply
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\lim\limits_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 = 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Next, using $x^{k+1} - y^k = -\eta (\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k)$ from \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2} and the second bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof3}, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof6}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k^2\Vertx^{k+1} - y^k\Vert^2 = \eta^2 \sum_{k=0}^{\infty} t_k^2 \Vert\hat{w}^{k+1} - \hat{w}^k\Vert^2 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Using again \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof4}, by Young's inequality and $\beta_k \leq \frac{\eta}{2}$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{2r^2}{t_k}\Vertz^k - x^k\Vert^2 + 2t_k(\eta - \beta_k)^2\Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2 \leq \frac{2r^2}{t_k}\Vertz^k - x^k\Vert^2 + 2\eta^2 t_k \Vert\hat{w}^k\Vert^2, \vspace{1ex}\\
		t_k \Vertx^{k+1} - x^k\Vert^2 &\leq& 2t_k\Vertx^{k+1} - y^k\Vert^2 + 2t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using the second summability bound in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2}, \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}, and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof6}, we can derive from the last inequalities that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th1_proof7}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Verty^k - x^k\Vert^2 < +\infty
		\text{\quad and \quad}
		\sum_{k=0}^\infty t_k \Vertx^{k+1} - x^k\Vert^2 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Now, since $\mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2} < +\infty$, from \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est3} of Lemma~\ref{le:FKM_key_est3}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\frac{\delta \eta}{2(r-1)}(t_k-r)^2 \Vertw^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2}{2\nu}\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \frac{d_k}{2}\VertGy^{k-1} - Gx^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \mathcal{P}_k \leq \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2} < +\infty,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	which implies that $\Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \leq M := \frac{\nu \mathcal{R}_0^2}{r^2}$.
	Using this bound, Young's inequality, and $\nu_k \leq \nu$, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.1em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		-\frac{2\nu_k}{r} \langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle &\leq& \frac{\nu_k t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu_k}{rt_k^2}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{\star}\Vert^2 \leq \frac{\nu t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{M\nu}{rt_k^2}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		-\frac{2\nu_k}{r} \langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\rangle &\leq& \frac{\nu_k t_k}{r} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu_k}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2 \leq \frac{\nu t_k}{r} \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using the last line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, $\nu_k \leq \nu$, and the last two relations, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{\star}\Vert^2 &=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + 2\langle z^{k+1} - z^k, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \Vertz^{k+1} - z^k\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1} + e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{\nu_k^2}{r^2}\Vertw^{k+1} + e^{k+1}\Vert^2 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, x^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle - \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle w^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\rangle\vspace{1ex}\\
		&& - {~} \frac{2\nu_k}{r}\langle e^{k+1}, z^{k+1} - x^{\star}\rangle  \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq&  \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2 + \left(\frac{2\nu}{r} + \frac{\nu t_k}{r}\right) \Vertw^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu}{rt_k}\Vertz^{k+1} - x^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{\nu t_k^2}{r}\Verte^{k+1}\Vert^2 + \frac{M\nu}{rt_k^2}.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since the last four terms on the right-hand side are summable due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof2} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5}, applying Lemma~\ref{le:A1}, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2$ exists.
	However, since $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert^2 = 0$ due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1}, using the triangle inequality as $|\Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert - \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert| \leq \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert$, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2$ also exists.
<!-- end head-4 -->
<!-- head-5: body_md -->
We have proved in \textbf{Step 4} that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - x^{\star}\Vert$ exists for every $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$. 
	Moreover, from the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof1}, we also have $\lim_{k \to \infty} \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert = 0$ for some $\xi^k \in Tx^k$, which means that $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertw^k\Vert = 0$ for $(x^k, w^k) \in \mathrm{gra}(\Phi)$.
	Applying Opial's lemma, we conclude that $\{x^k\}$ converges  to a solution $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	Next, since $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - z^k\Vert = 0$ and $\lim_{k \to \infty} \Vertx^k - y^k\Vert = 0$ due to \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof5.1} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_proof7}, we conclude that $\{y^k\}$ and $\{z^k\}$ also converge  to a solution $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	Finally, from the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, we can also show that $\{\hat{x}^k\}$ converges to $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
<!-- end head-5 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{co:FKM_scheme3_convergence}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM_assumption} holds for \eqref{eq:GE}, $\mathrm{zer}(\Phi) \neq \emptyset$, and $t_0$ satisfies \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0}. 
	Let $\{(x^k, y^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme3} where the parameters are chosen as
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_scheme3_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-2ex}
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 2, \quad \delta \in (0, r-2), \quad \omega := r^2 - 3r + 3,
				\quad
			0 < \eta \leq \frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+\omega}L}, 
			\vspace{1ex}\\
			0 < \nu < (r-2-\delta)\eta, \qquad t_{k} := k + t_0,  \qquad \theta_k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_{k+1}}, \qquad \hat{\lambda}_k := \eta\theta_k, \vspace{1ex}\\
			\hat{\eta}_k := \frac{[2(r-1)-\delta]\eta(t_{k+1} - r)}{2(r-1)t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}} + \eta, \quad \text{and} \quad
			\hat{\gamma}_k := \frac{[4(r-1) - \delta]\eta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_{k+1}} + \eta.
		\end{array}
		\hspace{-2ex}
	\end{equation}
	Then, all the $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates} still hold.
	Moreover, the sequences $\{x^k\}$ and $\{y^k\}$ converge to a solution $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Utilizing the parameters in \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_params}, we can compute that $\hat{\eta}_k = \frac{[2(r-1) - \delta]\eta (t_{k+1} - r)}{2(r-1) t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}} + \eta$, and $\hat{\gamma}_k = \frac{[4(r-1) - \delta]\eta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_{k+1}} + \eta$,  which proves \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme3_params}.
Then, since \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme3} is equivalent to \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, all the results of Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence} still hold.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- assumption-4: body_md -->
\label{as:FKM2_cocoercive}
	For problem \eqref{eq:GE}, there exist $L > 0$ and $\rho \geq 0$ with $L\rho < 1$ such that:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] (\textbf{Co-coercivity}) $G$ is $\frac{1}{L}$-co-coercive.
		\item[(ii)] (\textbf{Maximal co-hypomonotonicity}) $T$ is maximally $\rho$-co-hypomonotone.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end assumption-4 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{le:FB_cocoercive}
Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM2_cocoercive} holds for \eqref{eq:GE}.
Then, for any $\eta$ such that $\frac{2(1 - \sqrt{1 - L\rho})}{L} < \eta < \frac{2(1 + \sqrt{1 - L\rho})}{L}$, $\mathcal{G}_{\eta}$ defined by \eqref{eq:FBS_mapping} is $\bar{\beta}$-co-coercive with $\bar{\beta} := \frac{\eta (4 - L\eta) - 4\rho}{4(1 - L\rho)} > 0$.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{le:FKM2_key_est1}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM2_cocoercive} holds for \eqref{eq:GE}, $\mathrm{zer}(\Phi) \neq \emptyset$, and \eqref{eq:t0_cond2} holds.
	Let
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_key_est1_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			r > 1, \quad t_{k+1} = t_k + 1 , \quad 2\rho < \eta < 2\bar{\beta}, \quad 0 < \nu < (r-1)\eta, \quad \text{and} \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, $\mathcal{L}_k$ defined in \eqref{eq:FKM2_Lyapunov_function} with $\beta := \frac{\eta}{2}$ satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_key_est1}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{L}_k - \mathcal{L}_{k+1} &\geq& \left[ \frac{(r-1)\eta - \nu}{2}t_k + r(r-1)\bar{\beta} \right] \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 + \frac{(2\bar{\beta} - \eta) t_{k+1}(t_{k+1} - r)}{2} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^{k+1} - \mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2\vspace{1ex}\\
			&& + {~} \frac{r^2(r-1)}{2\nu t_k (t_k - 1)} \Vertz^{k+1} - x^{\star}\Vert^2.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{le:FKM2_key_est2}
	Under the same settings as in Lemma~\ref{le:FKM2_key_est1},  $\mathcal{L}_k$ in \eqref{eq:FKM2_Lyapunov_function} satisfies
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_key_est2}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{L}_k &\geq& \frac{A_k}{4} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2[((r-1)\eta  - 2\nu) t_k + 2\nu]}{2\nu\eta(t_k - 1)} \Vertz^k - x^{\star}\Vert^2,
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $A_k := \eta(2a_k - t_k^2) + 4r\bar{\beta}t_k \geq \eta t_k^2$.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{th:FKM2_convergence}
	For \eqref{eq:GE}, suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM2_cocoercive} holds, $\bar{\beta}$ is given in Lemma~\ref{le:FB_cocoercive}, and $t_0$ satisfies \eqref{eq:t0_cond2}. 
	Let $\{ (x^k, y^k, z^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM2_scheme_reformulation} using the following parameters
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_th2_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			r > 1, \quad t_{k} := k + t_0 , \quad 2\rho < \eta < 2\bar{\beta}, \quad 0 < \nu < (r-1)\eta, \quad \text{and} \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{itemize}
	\itemsep=-0.3em
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$]~\textbf{\textit{$($The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ rates$)$}} 
	There exist $\xi^k \in Tx^k$ for all $k \geq 0$ such that:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_BigO_convergence}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 \leq \dfrac{2\mathcal{R}_0^2}{\eta (k + t_0 - 1)^2} \quad \text{and} \quad \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 \leq \dfrac{2(1+L\eta)^2 \mathcal{R}_0^2}{\eta (k + t_0 - 1)^2},
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $\mathcal{R}_0^2 :=  \eta t_0 (t_0 - r) \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)t_0}{\nu (t_0 - 1)}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2$.
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$]~\textbf{\textit{$($The $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ rates$)$}}
	There exist $\xi^k \in Tx^k$ for all $k \geq 0$ such that:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_SmallO_convergence} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\lim_{k \to \infty} k^2 \VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 = 0 \qquad \text{and} \qquad
		\lim_{k \to \infty} k^2 \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(iii)}$]~\textbf{$($The convergence of iterates$)$} 
	All $\{x^k\}$, $\{y^k\}$, and $\{z^k\}$ converge to $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
\noindent$\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{\textit{$($The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates$)$.}}
	First, telescoping \eqref{eq:FKM2_key_est1} from $k:=0$ to $k := K$, then taking the limit of the resulting inequality as $K \to \infty$ and noticing that $\mathcal{L}_k \geq 0$, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_convergence1_proof1}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{L}_k &\leq& \mathcal{L}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty \left[ \frac{(r-1)\eta - \nu}{2}t_k + r(r-1)\bar{\beta} \right] \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \mathcal{L}_0, \vspace{1ex}\\
			\sum_{k=0}^\infty \frac{(2\bar{\beta} - \eta) t_{k+1}(t_{k+1} - r)}{2} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^{k+1} - \mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \mathcal{L}_0.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Since $x^0 = z^0 = y^0 = y^{-1}$, $\nu_0 = \frac{\nu(t_0 - 1)}{t_0}$, and $\mathcal{R}_0$ given in \eqref{eq:FKM2_BigO_convergence}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{L}_0 = \frac{\eta t_0 (t_0 - r)}{2} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^0\Vert^2 + \frac{r^2(r-1)t_0}{2\nu (t_0 - 1)}\Vertx^0 - x^{\star}\Vert^2 = \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Substituting this expression into the last two lines of \eqref{eq:FKM2_convergence1_proof1}, we obtain
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_summability_bounds}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0 - 1) \VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{(r-1)\eta - \nu}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k + t_0) (k + t_0 - r)\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k - \mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^{k-1}\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{\mathcal{R}_0^2}{2\bar{\beta} - \eta}.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Now, since $\hat{w}^{k+1} = Gy^k + \xi^{k+1}$, we have $\xi^{k+1} = \hat{w}^{k+1} - Gy^k = \mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k - Gy^k \in Tx^{k+1}$.
	Moreover, since $G$ is $\frac{1}{L}$-co-coercive, it is also $L$-Lipschitz continuous.
	Using the triangle inequality, the $L$-Lipschitz continuity of $G$, and the second line of \eqref{eq:FKM2_scheme_reformulation}, we can show that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM2_convergence1_proof2}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\VertGx^{k+1} + \xi^{k+1}\Vert &=& \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k + (Gx^{k+1} - Gy^k)\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\leq& \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert + \VertGx^{k+1} - Gy^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\leq& \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert + L\Vertx^{k+1} - y^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&=& (1 + L\eta) \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert = (1 + L\eta) \VertGy^{k-1} + \xi^k\Vert.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Putting this relation into the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM2_summability_bounds}, we get  
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\sum_{k=1}^\infty (k+t_0) \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \frac{(1+L\eta)^2\mathcal{R}_0^2}{(r-1)\eta - \nu},
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Moreover, from \eqref{eq:FKM2_key_est2}, we also have
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-1ex}\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{L}_k \geq \frac{A_k}{4} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 \geq \frac{\eta t_k^2}{4} \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 = \frac{\eta (k + t_0)^2}{4}\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2. 
		\end{array}\hspace{-1ex}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining the last two expressions and the first line of \eqref{eq:FKM2_convergence1_proof1}, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-1ex}\begin{array}{lcl}
			\eta (k + t_0)^2\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\eta}y^k\Vert^2 \leq 4 \mathcal{L}_k \leq 4 \mathcal{L}_0 = 2\mathcal{R}_0^2,
		\end{array}\hspace{-1ex}
	\end{equation*}
	which proves the first bound of \eqref{eq:FKM2_BigO_convergence}.
	Utilizing this bound and \eqref{eq:FKM2_convergence1_proof2}, we obtain the second bound of  \eqref{eq:FKM2_BigO_convergence}.
	
	\vspace{1ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(ii)}$ and $\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{\textit{$($The $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ rates and convergence of iterates$)$.}}
	The proof of the claims in (ii) and (iii) is very similar to those in Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence}.
	Thus, we omit them here.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{co:FKM_scheme2+_convergence}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:FKM2_cocoercive} holds for \eqref{eq:GE}, $\mathrm{zer}(\Phi) \neq\emptyset$, and $t_0$ satisfies \eqref{eq:t0_cond2}. 
	Let $\{(x^k, y^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2+} where the parameters are chosen as
	\begin{equation*} 
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 1, \qquad t_k = k + t_0, \qquad 2\rho < \eta < \frac{2}{L}, \qquad 0 < \nu < (r-1)\eta, 
			\vspace{1ex}\\
			\theta_k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_{k+1}}, \qquad  \hat{\eta}_k = \frac{\eta (t_{k+1} - r)}{t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu (t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}}, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \hat{\gamma}_k = \eta \theta_k.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Then, all the $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ convergence rates in \eqref{eq:FKM2_BigO_convergence} and \eqref{eq:FKM2_SmallO_convergence} still hold.
	Moreover, $\{x^k\}$ and $\{y^k\}$ converge to $x^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
Since \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2+} is equivalent to \eqref{eq:FKM2_scheme}, this corollary follows directly from Theorem~\ref{th:FKM2_convergence}.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{le:3op_inclusion_assumptions}
	For \eqref{eq:3op_inclusion}, suppose that $A: \mathbb{R}^p \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^p$ and $C: \mathbb{R}^p \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^p$ are maximally monotone, and $B : \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}^p$ is single-valued.
    Suppose further that $\tau > 0$ and $\sigma > 0$ in matrix $P$ from \eqref{eq:3op_P} satisfy $\tau \sigma < 1$.
	Then, for $T$ and $G$ defined by \eqref{eq:3op_reform2}, the following statements hold.
	\begin{itemize}
	\itemsep=-0.3em
		\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] 
		The mapping $P^{-1}T: \mathbb{R}^{2p} \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^{2p}$ is maximally monotone w.r.t. $\langle \cdot, \cdot\rangle_P$.
		
		\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] 
		If $B$ is monotone and $L_B$-Lipschitz continuous, then $P^{-1}G$ is monotone w.r.t. $\langle \cdot, \cdot\rangle_P$ and $L_G$-Lipschitz continuous w.r.t. $\Vert\cdot\Vert_P$, where $L_G := \frac{\tau L_B}{1 - \tau \sigma}$.
		
		\item[$\mathrm{(iii)}$] 
		If $B$ is $\frac{1}{L_B}$-co-coercive, then $P^{-1}G$ is $\frac{1}{L_G}$-co-coercive w.r.t. $\Vert\cdot\Vert_P$, where $L_G := \frac{\tau L_B}{1 - \tau \sigma}$.		
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- head-6: body_md -->
Update
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\hat{u}^k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} u^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_u^k 
			\qquad \text{and} \qquad 
			\hat{v}^k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} v^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_v^k.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-6 -->
<!-- head-7: body_md -->
For $\hat{w}^k = P^{-1}Gy^{k-1} + \xi^k$ with $\xi^k \in P^{-1}Tx^k$, we define $\tilde{w}^k = P\hat{w}^k = Gy^{k-1} + P\xi^k$.
	Then, using $P^{-1} = \frac{1}{1 - \tau \sigma} \begin{bmatrix}
		\tau \mathbb{I} & \tau \sigma \mathbb{I} \\ 
		\tau \sigma \mathbb{I} & \sigma \mathbb{I}
	\end{bmatrix}$, we have $\hat{w}^k = P^{-1}\tilde{w}^k = \frac{1}{1 - \tau\sigma} \begin{bmatrix}
		\tau \tilde{w}_u^k + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_v^k \\
		\tau \sigma \tilde{w}_u^k + \sigma \tilde{w}_v^k
	\end{bmatrix}$.
	Then, we update
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			y_u^k = \hat{u}^k - \frac{\gamma_k - \beta_k}{1 - \tau\sigma} (\tau \tilde{w}_u^k + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_v^k). 
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-7 -->
<!-- head-8: body_md -->
From the third line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, we have
	$x^{k+1} = \hat{x}^k - (P^{-1}Gy^k + \xi^{k+1}) + \beta_k \hat{w}^k$.
	Using the definitions of $G$, $T$, and $P$, we can derive from the last relation that
	\begin{equation*}
		\begin{cases}
			u^{k+1} = J_{\tau A} (\hat{u}^k - \tau \hat{v}^k - \tau By_u^k + \tau \beta_k \tilde{w}_u^k) \vspace{1ex}\\
			v^{k+1} = J_{\sigma C^{-1}} (\hat{v}^k + \sigma (2u^{k+1} - \hat{u}^k) + \sigma \beta_k \tilde{w}_v^k)
		\end{cases}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $C$ is maximally monotone, we have the inverse-resolvent identity $J_{\sigma C^{-1}}(x) = x - \sigma J_{\sigma^{-1} C} \left(\frac{x}{\sigma}\right)$.
	Thus,  the update of $v^{k+1}$ can be expressed as
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\begin{cases}
				\tilde{v}^{k+1} = \hat{v}^k + \sigma (2u^{k+1} - \hat{u}^k) + \sigma \beta_k \tilde{w}_v^k \vspace{1ex}\\
				v^{k+1} = \tilde{v}^{k+1} - \sigma J_{\sigma^{-1} C} (\frac{1}{\sigma} \tilde{v}^{k+1}).
			\end{cases}
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-8 -->
<!-- head-9: body_md -->
From the third line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we have $\hat{w}^{k+1} = \hat{x}^k - x^{k+1} + \beta_k \hat{w}^k$.
	Multiplying both sides of this equation by $P$ and using $\tilde{w}^k = P\hat{w}^k$, we get $\tilde{w}^{k+1} = P(\hat{x}^k - x^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}^k$, which leads to the following updates
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\left\{\begin{array}{lcl}
				\tilde{w}_u^{k+1} & = &  \frac{1}{\tau} (\hat{u}^k - u^{k+1}) - (\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_u^k \vspace{1ex}\\ 
				\tilde{w}_v^{k+1}  & = & - (\hat{u}^k - u^{k+1}) + \frac{1}{\sigma} (\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_v^k.
		\end{array}\right.
	\end{equation*}
	\item
<!-- end head-9 -->
<!-- head-10: body_md -->
Using this $\tilde{w}^{k+1}$, we have $\hat{w}^{k+1} = P^{-1}\tilde{w}^{k+1} = \frac{1}{1 - \tau\sigma} \begin{bmatrix}
		\tau \tilde{w}_u^{k+1} + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_v^{k+1} \\
		\tau \sigma \tilde{w}_u^{k+1} + \sigma \tilde{w}_v^{k+1}
	\end{bmatrix}$.
	Then, from the fourth line of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}, we update
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			z_u^{k+1} = z_u^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r} \hat{w}_u^{k+1} 
			\qquad \text{and} \qquad
			z_v^{k+1} = z_v^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r} \hat{w}_v^{k+1}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
\end{compactitem}
<!-- end head-10 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}
	For Inclusion \eqref{eq:3op_inclusion}, suppose that $A$ and $C$ are maximally monotone and $B$ is monotone and $L_B$-Lipschitz continuous.
	Suppose further that $\mathrm{zer}(A+B+C) \ne \emptyset$ and $t_0$ is given by \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0}.
	Let $\{(u^k, \hat{u}^k, y_u^k, z_u^k)\}$ and $\{(v^k, \hat{v}^k, z_v^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme} where the parameters are chosen as
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_3OP_th1_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 2, \quad \delta \in (0,r-2), \quad \omega := r^2 - 3r + 3, \quad 0 < \nu < r - 2 - \delta, \vspace{1ex}\\
			2\tau L_B\sqrt{1+\omega} + \tau \sigma \leq 1, \quad t_{k} := k + t_0, \quad \beta_k := \frac{\delta (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_k}, \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}, \quad \gamma_k := 1.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{itemize}
	\itemsep=-0.3em 
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] $\mathrm{(\text{\textbf{The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ rates}})}$ 
	For $\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}$ defined by \eqref{eq:FBS_residual3},  $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rates of $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$.
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] $\mathrm{(\text{\textbf{The convergence of the iterates}})}$ 
	The iterate sequences  $\{u^k\}$, $\{\hat{u}^k\}$, $\{y_u^k\}$, and $\{z_u^k\}$ all converge  to $u^{\star}$, and the iterate sequences $\{v^k\}$, $\{\hat{v}^k\}$, and $\{z_v^k\}$ all converge to $v^{\star}$, where $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(A+B+C)$.
	Consequently, all these sequences are bounded.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
First, since $2\tau L_B\sqrt{1+\omega} + \tau \sigma \leq 1$, we can show that $1 \leq \frac{1 - \tau \sigma}{2\sqrt{1+\omega}\tau L_B} = \frac{1}{2\sqrt{1+\omega}L_G}$.
	Thus, all the conditions in \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_th1_params} are satisfied for $\eta = 1$ by \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_params}.
	
	Now, for $x^k = [u^k,  v^k]$, by definition, we have $Gx^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		Bu^k \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix}$ and $Tx^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		Au^k + v^k \\ C^{-1}v^k - u^k
	\end{bmatrix}$.
	Take $\zeta^k \in Au^k$ and $\vartheta^k \in C^{-1}v^k$, then we have $\xi^k := \begin{bmatrix}
		\zeta^k + v^k \\ \vartheta^k - u^k
	\end{bmatrix} \in Tx^k$, and thus $Gx^k + \xi^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k \\ \vartheta^k - u^k
	\end{bmatrix}$, leading to
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof1}
		\VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert^2 + \Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert^2.
	\end{equation}
	Since \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme} is equivalent to \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme} (or \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme2}), applying \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_BigO_rates} and \eqref{eq:FKM_th1_SmallO_rates}, we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{ccc}
			\VertP^{-1}Gx^k + P^{-1}\xi^k\Vert_P^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big) \quad \text{and} \quad \VertP^{-1}Gx^k + P^{-1}\xi^k\Vert_P^2 = o\big(1/k^2\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Applying Lemma~\ref{le:norm_equivalence}, we can derive that
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{ccc}
			\VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big) \quad \text{and} \quad \VertGx^k + \xi^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k^2\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining these results and \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof1}, we obtain	
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof2}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{ccc}
			\Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k^2\big), \vspace{1ex}\\
			\Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k^2\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Now, since $\vartheta^k \in C^{-1}v^k$, we have $v^k \in C\vartheta^k$, or equivalently, $\vartheta^k = J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(\vartheta^k + \sigma^{-1}v^k)$.
	Let us recall the FBS residual from \eqref{eq:FBS_residual3}:
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k &:=& \sigma \left(u^k - J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(u^k - \sigma^{-1} (\zeta^k + Bu^k))\right) \vspace{1ex}\\
			&=& \sigma (u^k - \vartheta^k) + \sigma\left(\vartheta^k - J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(u^k - \sigma^{-1} (\zeta^k + Bu^k))\right).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	By the nonexpansiveness of $J_{\sigma^{-1}C}$ and the triangle inequality, we can easily show that
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\Vert\vartheta^k - J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(u^k - \sigma^{-1} (\zeta^k + Bu^k))\Vert &=& \VertJ_{\sigma^{-1}C}(\vartheta^k + \sigma^{-1}v^k) -  J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(u^k - \sigma^{-1} (\zeta^k + Bu^k))\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\leq& \Vert\vartheta^k + \sigma^{-1}v^k - u^k + \sigma^{-1} (\zeta^k + Bu^k)\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
			&\leq& \Vertu^k - \vartheta^k\Vert + \sigma^{-1}\Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using this bound and triangle inequality, we can derive that 
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k\Vert &\leq& 2\sigma \Vertu^k - \vartheta^k\Vert + \Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this relation and \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof2}, we get
	\begin{equation*} 
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{ccc}
			\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k^2\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, let $x^{\star} = [u^{\star}, v^{\star}]$ be a solution of \eqref{eq:3op_inclusion3}.
	By the definitions of $G$ and $T$, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof4}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{ccc}
			0 \in Au^{\star} + Bu^{\star} + v^{\star} \quad &\text{and}& \quad 0 \in C^{-1}v^{\star} - u^{\star}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	From the second inclusion of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof4}, we get $v^{\star} \in Cu^{\star}$.
	Substituting this relation into the first inclusion of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof4} yields $0 \in Au^{\star} + Bu^{\star} + Cu^{\star}$, which implies that $u^{\star}$ is a solution to \eqref{eq:3op_inclusion}.
	Thus, the convergence of the iterates generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme} follows directly from part (iii) of Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence}.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{th:FKM_3OP_convergence2}
	For Inclusion \eqref{eq:3op_inclusion}, suppose that $A$ and $C$ are maximally monotone  and $B$ is $\frac{1}{L_B}$-co-coercive.
	Suppose further that $\mathrm{zer}(A+B+C) \ne \emptyset$ and $t_0$ is given by \eqref{eq:t0_cond2}.
	Let $\{(u^k, \hat{u}^k, z_u^k)\}$ and $\{(v^k, \hat{v}^k, z_v^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme2} where the parameters are chosen as
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:FKM_3OP_th2_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 1, \quad 0 < \nu < r - 1, \quad \frac{1}{2}\tau L_B + \tau \sigma < 1, \quad t_{k} := k + t_0, \quad \text{and} \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold. 
	\begin{itemize}
	\itemsep=-0.3em
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] $\mathrm{(\text{\textbf{The $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$ rates}})}$ 
	For $\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}$ defined by \eqref{eq:FBS_residual3}, $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{-1}}u^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rates of $\mathcal{O}\big(1/k^2\big)$ and $o\big(1/k^2\big)$.
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] $\mathrm{(\text{\textbf{The convergence of the iterates}})}$ 
		The iterate sequences $\{u^k\}$, $\{\hat{u}^k\}$, and $\{z_u^k\}$ all converge  to $u^{\star}$ and the iterate sequences $\{v^k\}$, $\{\hat{v}^k\}$, and $\{z_v^k\}$ all converge to $v^{\star}$, where $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(A+B+C)$.
		Consequently, all these sequences are bounded.
	\end{itemize}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Since $\frac{1}{2}\tau L_B + \tau \sigma < 1$, we have $1 < \frac{2(1 - \tau\sigma)}{\tau L_B} = \frac{2}{L_G}$.
	Thus, all the conditions in \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_th2_params} are satisfied for $\eta = 1$ by \eqref{eq:FKM_th2_params}.
	The remainder of this proof is very similar to that of Theorem~\ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}.
	Thus, we omit it here.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{le:consensus}
$\mathbf{u}^{\star} = (u_{[1]}^{\star}, \cdots, u_{[n]}^{\star})^\top$ is a solution of \eqref{eq:GE2} if and only if $u_{[1]}^{\star} = \cdots = u_{[n]}^{\star}$ and $u^{\star} := u_{[1]}^{\star}$ is a solution of \eqref{eq:DGE}.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- head-11: body_md -->
Applying directly \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme3} with  stepsize $\eta = 1$ to \eqref{eq:precond_GE}, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_scheme0}
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\left\{
\begin{array}{lcl}
	\mathbf{x}^{k+1} &=& \mathbf{y}^k - \hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}^k, \quad \boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1} \in \mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^{k+1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
	\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} &=& \mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{G}\mathbf{y}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
	\mathbf{y}^{k+1} &=& \mathbf{y}^k + \theta_k \left(\mathbf{y}^k - \mathbf{y}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}^k  - \hat{\lambda}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k-1}.
\end{array}
\right.
\end{equation}
\item
<!-- end head-11 -->
<!-- head-12: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{x}^k :=  [\mathbf{u}^k,  \mathbf{v}^k]$ and  $\mathbf{y}^k := [\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k, \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k]$. 
Since $\boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1} \in \mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^{k+1}$, we have $\mathbb{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1} \in \mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^{k+1}$. 
From the second line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme0}, we have $\mathbb{P}\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} = \mathbb{G}\mathbf{y}^k + \mathbb{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1}$.
From the first line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme0}, we can derive that $\mathbb{P} \hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} = \mathbb{P}\mathbf{y}^k - \mathbb{P}\mathbf{x}^{k+1} + \gamma_k \mathbb{P}\hat{\mathbf{w}}^k$.
Combining these two relations and $\mathbb{P}\boldsymbol{\xi}^{k+1} \in \mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^{k+1}$, we obtain
\begin{equation*} 
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\begin{array}{lcl}
	\mathbb{P}\mathbf{y}^k - \mathbb{G}\mathbf{y}^k + \gamma_k \mathbb{P}\hat{\mathbf{w}}^k &\in& \mathbb{P}\mathbf{x}^{k+1} + \mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^{k+1}.
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Using the definition of $\mathbb{G}$, $\mathbb{T}$, and $\mathbb{P}$, we can derive from the last inclusion that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_derivation1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\frac{1}{\tau} \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \gamma_k (\frac{1}{\tau}\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k) &\in& \frac{1}{\tau}\mathbf{u}^{k+1} + \mathbf{T}\mathbf{u}^{k+1} + 2 \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^{k+1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{K} \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \frac{1}{\sigma} \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k + \gamma_k (\mathbf{K} \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k + \frac{1}{\sigma}\hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k) &=& \frac{1}{\sigma}\mathbf{v}^{k+1}.
	\end{array}
	\right.
\end{equation}
\item
<!-- end head-12 -->
<!-- head-13: body_md -->
Denote $\mathbf{s}^k := \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k$.
Then, we can derive from the second line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_derivation1} that 
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{v}^{k+1} &=& \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k + \sigma \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k.
	\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Substituting this into the first line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_derivation1}, then rearranging the result, we obtain
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \gamma_k (\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k) -  2 \tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k&\in& \mathbf{u}^{k+1} + \tau \mathbf{T}\mathbf{u}^{k+1},
	\end{array}
\end{equation*}
which is equivalent to 
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \gamma_k (\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k) -  2\tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\mathbf{s}^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \tau \gamma_k  \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k -  2 \tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\right).
	\end{array}
\end{equation*}
\item
<!-- end head-13 -->
<!-- head-14: body_md -->
Putting the relations of $\mathbf{s}^k$, $\mathbf{v}^{k+1}$, $\mathbf{u}^{k+1}$, and \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme0} together, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_derivation2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{s}^k &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{v}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k + \sigma \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &:=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\mathbf{s}^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \tau \gamma_k  \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k -  2\tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \mathbf{u}^{k+1}  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \mathbf{v}^{k+1}  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \theta_k \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k  - \hat{\lambda}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k-1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k + \theta_k \left(\hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \hat{\mathbf{v}}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k  - \hat{\lambda}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^{k-1}.
	\end{array}
	\right.
\end{equation}
Substituting $\mathbf{v}^{k+1} = \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k + \sigma \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k$ into the fifth line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_derivation2}, we get
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^{k+1} &=& -\sigma \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k.
	\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Putting this expression into the third line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_derivation2}, we obtain
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &:=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\mathbf{s}^k - \tau \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \tau \sigma \gamma_k  \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^{k-1} -  2\tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
	\end{array}
\end{equation*}
Using the transformation $\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k := \mathbf{K}^\top \hat{\mathbf{v}}^k$ and eliminating $\mathbf{v}^k$ and $\hat{\mathbf{w}}_v^k$ from \eqref{eq:DFKM_derivation2}, we have
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{s}^k &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &:=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\mathbf{s}^k - \tau \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \tau \sigma \gamma_k  \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^{k-1} -  2 \tau \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \mathbf{u}^{k+1} + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \theta_k \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k  - \hat{\lambda}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k-1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k+1} &:=& \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k + \theta_k \left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k-1} \right) + \sigma \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K} (\hat{\eta}_k\mathbf{s}^k - \hat{\gamma}_k \mathbf{s}^{k-1}  + \hat{\lambda}_k \mathbf{s}^{k-2}).
	\end{array}
	\right.
\end{equation*}
\item
<!-- end head-14 -->
<!-- head-15: body_md -->
Observe that $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K}$ is only multiplied by $\mathbf{s}$.
Therefore, if we define $\mathbf{d}^k := \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K} \mathbf{s}^k$, then the last scheme becomes
\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathbf{s}^k &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{d}^k &:=& \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K} \mathbf{s}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &:=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\mathbf{s}^k - \tau \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \tau \sigma \gamma_k  \mathbf{d}^{k-1} -  2\tau \sigma \mathbf{d}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \mathbf{u}^{k+1}  + \gamma_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \theta_k \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k  - \hat{\lambda}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k-1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k+1} &:=& \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k + \theta_k \left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k-1} \right) + \sigma (\hat{\eta}_k\mathbf{d}^k - \hat{\gamma}_k \mathbf{d}^{k-1}  + \hat{\lambda}_k \mathbf{d}^{k-2}).
	\end{array}
	\right.
\end{equation*}
\item
<!-- end head-15 -->
<!-- head-16: body_md -->
We choose the matrix $\mathbf{K}$ such that $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{K} = \frac{\mathbb{I} - W}{2}$.
In this case, the condition $\sigma\tau\Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert^2 < 1$ is equivalent to $\sigma\tau\Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert < 2$.
<!-- end head-16 -->
<!-- assumption-5: body_md -->
\label{as:DGE_monotone_Lipschitz}
	The distributed composite inclusion \eqref{eq:DGE} satisfies the following conditions: 
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] (\textbf{Local maximal monotonicity}) $\Phi_i := G_i + T_i$ is maximally monotone for all $i \in [n]$.
		\item[(ii)] (\textbf{Local Lipschitz continuity}) $G_i$ is $L$-Lipschitz continuous for all $i \in [n]$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end assumption-5 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{le:DGE_assumption}
	Suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_monotone_Lipschitz} holds for \eqref{eq:DGE} and $\tau\sigma\Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert < 2$.  
	Then
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] 
	$\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{G}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is $\tilde{L}$-Lipschitz continuous w.r.t.  $\Vert\cdot\Vert_{\mathbb{P}}$, where $\tilde{L} := \frac{2\tau L}{2 - \tau \sigma \Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert}$.
	
	\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] 
	$\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{G} + \mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{T}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is maximally monotone w.r.t. $\langle \cdot, \cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{P}}$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{th:DFKM_convergence}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_monotone_Lipschitz} holds. 
	Let $\{ (\mathbf{u}^k, \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k) \}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme} using $t_0$ given in \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0} and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-2ex}
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 2, \qquad \delta \in (0, r-2), \qquad \omega := r^2 - 3r + 3, \qquad 0 < \nu < r-2-\delta,
			\vspace{1ex}\\
			2\tau L\sqrt{1+\omega} + \frac{1}{2}\tau \sigma (1 - \lambda_{\min}(W)) \leq 1, 
			\qquad 
			t_{k} := k + t_0, 
			\qquad 
			\gamma_k := 1, \vspace{1ex}\\
			\hat{\eta}_k := \frac{[2(r-1)-\delta](t_{k+1} - r)}{2(r-1)t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}} + 1, \quad
			\hat{\gamma}_k := \frac{[4(r-1) - \delta] (t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_{k+1}} + 1, \  \text{and} \
			\hat{\lambda}_k = \theta_k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_{k+1}}.
		\end{array}
		\hspace{-2ex}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{$($Local iterates$)$}]
	The local iterates $\{u_{[i]}^k\}$ and $\{\hat{u}_{[i]}^k\}$ converge to the same $u^\star \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$ of the original \eqref{eq:DGE} problem for all nodes $i \in [n]$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{$($Consensus errors$)$}]
	The consensus errors $\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k \Vert$ and $\Vert\Pi \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k \Vert$ converge to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{$($Aggregated residual$)$}]
	The aggregated residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\xi_i^k \in Tu_{[i]}^k$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{$($Restricted gap$)$}]
	If, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then the restricted gap value $\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\bar{u}^k := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n u_{[i]}^k$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k)$ converges to zero at an $o\big(1/k\big)$ convergence rate, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{$($FBS residual$)$}]
	For a given $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{L})$, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at an $o\big(1/k\big)$ convergence rate.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at an $o\big(1/k\big)$ convergence rate, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{$($Special case$)$}]
	If $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$ $($i.e., \eqref{eq:DGE} reduces to \eqref{eq:DNE}$)$, then $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
First, we can show that
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert^2 = \Vert\mathbf{K}^2\Vert = \frac{1}{2}\Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert = \frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{I} - W) = \frac{1 - \lambda_{\min}(W)}{2} > 0,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	where we have used $\mathbb{I} \succeq W$ from Definition~\ref{def:mixing_matrix} in the last inequality.
	Using this relation, the condition $2\sqrt{1+\omega}\tau L + \frac{1}{2}\tau \sigma (1 - \lambda_{\min}(W)) \leq 1$ is equivalent to $2\sqrt{1+\omega}\tau L + \tau \sigma \Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert^2 \leq 1$, leading to $\tau \sigma \Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{2}\sigma\tau\Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert < 1$, and thus Lemma~\ref{le:DGE_assumption} holds.
	Moreover, we can easily show that the conditions in Corollary~\ref{co:FKM_scheme3_convergence} hold for $\eta = 1$.
	Therefore, all the results from Corollary~\ref{co:FKM_scheme3_convergence} (or Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence}) can be applied to Algorithm~\ref{alg:ND_DFFP}.
	
	                                                        
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{The convergence of the local iterate sequences.}
	By Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence}(iii), both sequences $\{\mathbf{x}^k\}$ and $\{\mathbf{y}^k\}$ converge to $\mathbf{x}^{\star} = [\mathbf{u}^{\star}, \mathbf{v}^{\star}]$, where $\mathbf{u^{\star}}$ is a solution of \eqref{eq:GE2}.
	By Lemma~\ref{le:consensus}, $\mathbf{u}^{\star} = [u^{\star}, \cdots, u^{\star}]^\top$ and $u^{\star}$ is a solution to \eqref{eq:DGE}.
