	Since $\mathbf{x}^k \to \mathbf{x}^{\star}$ and $\mathbf{y}^k \to \mathbf{x}^{\star}$ as $k \to \infty$, we obtain $\mathbf{u}^k \to \mathbf{u}^{\star}$ and $\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k \to \mathbf{u}^{\star}$, thus $u^k_{[i]} \to u^{\star}$ and $\hat{u}^k_{[i]} \to u^{\star}$ for all $i \in [n]$.
	
	                                            
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{The convergence of consensus errors.}
	For $\mathbbm{w}^k \in \mathbb{G}\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbb{T}\mathbf{x}^k$, from Theorem~\ref{th:FKM1_convergence}, we have $\Vert\mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert_{\mathbb{P}} = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big)$ and $\Vert\mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert_{\mathbb{P}} = o\big(1/k\big)$.
	By definition, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\Vert\mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert_{\mathbb{P}}^2 &=& \langle \mathbbm{w}^k, \mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbbm{w}^k\rangle \geq \lambda_{\min}(\mathbb{P}^{-1})\Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{P})} \Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert^2,
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	leading to $\Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert^2 \leq \lambda_{\max}(\mathbb{P})\Vert \mathbb{P}^{-1}\mathbbm{w}^k \Vert_{\mathbb{P}}^2$.
	Thus, we conclude that $\Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big)$ and $\Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big)$.
	However, since $\Vert\mathbbm{w}^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert^2 + \Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^k\Vert^2$ for $\boldsymbol{\xi}^k \in \mathbf{T}\mathbf{u}^k$, we also get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_th1_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) 
		\qquad &\text{and}& \qquad 
		\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big), \vspace{1ex}\\
		\Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) 
		\qquad & \text{and} & \qquad 
		\Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	By a similar argument as above, we can show that $\Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big)$ and $\Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big)$.
	However, since $\Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}_u^k\Vert \leq \Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}^k\Vert$, we also have $\Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}_u^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big)$ and $\Vert\hat{\mathbbm{w}}_u^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big)$.
	Then, we can derive from the fourth line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme} that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\mathbf{K}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k\Vert = \Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^{k+1} + \mathbf{K}(\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k)\Vert \leq \Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^{k+1}\Vert + \Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert\big(\Vert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^{k+1}\Vert + \Vert\hat{\mathbf{w}}_u^k\Vert\big)
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this bound with the above results, we obtain 
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{rcl}				
			\Vert\mathbf{K}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) 
			\qquad &\text{and}& \qquad 
			\Vert\mathbf{K}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	   
	Now, we can write $\mathbf{u}^k = (\mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k) + \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k$, where $\bar{u}^k := \frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^n u_{[i]}^k$.
	Take an arbitrary $\mathbf{s} = (s^\top, \cdots, s^\top)^\top \in \mathrm{Null}(\mathbf{K})$, then we have
	\begin{equation*}
		\langle \mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k, \mathbf{s}\rangle 
		= \sum_{i=1}^n \langle u_{[i]}^k - \bar{u}^k, s\rangle 
		= \left\langle \sum_{i=1}^n (u_{[i]}^k - \bar{u}^k), s\right\rangle 
		= \langle 0, s\rangle = 0.
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, $\mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k \in \mathrm{Null}(\mathbf{K})^{\perp}$.
	However,  one can easily prove that for any $\mathbf{s} \in \mathrm{Null}(\mathbf{K})^{\perp}$, we have $\Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}\Vert \geq \sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{K})\Vert\mathbf{s}\Vert$, where $\sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{K}) > 0$ is the smallest positive singular value of $\mathbf{K}$.
	Setting $\mathbf{s} := \mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
		\Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2 \leq \frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{K})^2}\Vert\mathbf{K}(\mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k)\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{K})^2}\Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{u}^k\Vert^2,
	\end{equation*}
	where the last equality holds because $\mathbf{K}\boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k = 0$ due to $\boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k \in \mathrm{Null}(\mathbf{K})$.
	Thus, we conclude that the consensus error also converges to zero at the rates
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert  = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Moreover, since $\Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\mathbf{u}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2 = \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertu_{[i]}^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert^2$, we also have
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{rcl}	
			\Vertu_{[i]}^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vertu_{[i]}^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big) \quad \text{for all $i \in [n]$}.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	The consensus of $\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k$ can also be obtained similarly:
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\Pi\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \Vert\Pi\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	                                                 
	 \vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{The convergence of aggregated residuals.}
	Let us denote by $\mathbf{w}_u := \mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$ for $\boldsymbol{\xi}^k \in \mathbf{T}\mathbf{u}^k$.
	Then, since $\boldsymbol{1}^\top \mathbf{K}^\top = (\mathbf{K} \boldsymbol{1}) ^\top = 0$, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\boldsymbol{1}^\top \mathbf{w}_u = \boldsymbol{1}^\top (\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k) + \boldsymbol{1}^\top \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k = \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k),
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	for $\xi_i^k \in T_i u_{[i]}^k$.
	Thus, by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert^2 = \Vert\boldsymbol{1}^\top \mathbf{w}_u\Vert^2 \leq \Vert\boldsymbol{1}\Vert^2 \Vert\mathbf{w}_u\Vert^2 = n\Vert\mathbf{w}_u\Vert^2 = n \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this relation and the first line of \eqref{eq:DFKM_th1_proof1}, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) 
		\qquad &\text{and}& \qquad 
		\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	                                                      
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{The convergence of the restricted gap values.}
	We consider two cases as follows.
	
	\noindent\textbf{Case $\mathrm{(i)}$.}~
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap},
	let us denote by $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) := \sup_{u \in {\mathcal{B}}} \mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k)$, 
	where $\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) := \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u\rangle$. 
	By inserting $u_{[i]}^k$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
	\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) = \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $G_i + T_i$ is monotone, for ${\xi}_i^k \in T_i {u}_i^k$, we have $\langle G_i u + \xi_i, {u}_i^k - u\rangle \leq \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - u\rangle$.
	Substituting this relation into the last inequality, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_i^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, \bar{u}^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_i^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \left\langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k), \bar{u}^k - u\right\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \left\langle (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k) - (G_i u + \xi_i), {u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\right\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n \left( \VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \right) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Taking supremum on both sides of this inequality over ${\mathcal{B}}$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k) &\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert \sup_{u \in B} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&& + {~} \sum_{i=1}^n \sup_{u \in B} \left( \VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \right) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\{u_i^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$ for all $i \in [n]$, $\bar{u}^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n u_i^k$ converges to $u^{\star}$, and thus there exists $M_1 > 0$ such that $\sup_{u \in {\mathcal{B}}} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert \leq M_1$.
	Next, we have $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k = (\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k) - \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$, where the first term $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$ converges to zero as proved in part (b) and the second term $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$ converges to $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^{\star}$, leading to the boundedness of $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k$.
	Thus, there exists $M_2 > 0$ such that $\VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert \leq M_2$ for all $i \in [n]$.
	Finally, since ${\mathcal{B}} \subset \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T_i))$, by Lemma~\ref{le:monotone_local_bounded}, there exists $M_3 > 0$ such that $\VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \leq M_3$ for all $i = 1, \cdots, n$.
	Substituting these bounds into the last inequality, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) &\leq& M_1 \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n (M_2 + M_3) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this relation and the results in part (ii) and (iii), we conclude that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k) = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	           
	\noindent\textbf{Case~$\mathrm{(ii)}$.}
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}, we have $\mathrm{dom}(T_i) = \mathcal{C}_i$, and thus $\mathcal{B} = \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathcal{C}_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, and $\bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathcal{C}_i\right) \ne \emptyset$.
	By \cite[Corollary 3]{bauschke1999strong}, the collection $\{\mathcal{C}_1, \cdots, \mathcal{C}_n\}$ is boundedly linear regular, i.e., for every bounded set $\mathcal{Q} \subseteq \mathbb{R}^p$, there exists $\kappa_{\mathcal{Q}} > 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
		\mathrm{dist}\left(u, \mathcal{B}\right) \leq \kappa_{\mathcal{Q}} \max_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(u, \mathcal{C}_i\right), \qquad u \in \mathcal{Q}.
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\bar{u}^k \to u^{\star}$, $\{\bar{u}^k\}_{k \geq 0}$ is bounded.
	Taking $\mathcal{Q} := \{\bar{u}^k\}_{k \geq 0}$.
	Then, there exists $\kappa > 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{B}\right) \leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{C}_i\right), \qquad \forall k \geq 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Next, let $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$, then we have $\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert = \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{B}\right)$.
	Since $u_{[i]}^k \in \mathrm{dom}(T_i) = \mathcal{C}_i$, we obtain from the last inequality that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert \leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{C}_i\right) 
		\leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert 
		= \kappa \Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert_{\infty} \leq \kappa \Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using this relation, triangle inequality, and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can derive that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\sum_{i=1}^n \Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \big(\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert\big) \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& n\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& n\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \sqrt{n}\big(\sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert^2\big)^{1/2} \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& (n\kappa + \sqrt{n})\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Now, fix arbitrary $u \in \mathcal{B}$ and arbitrary $\xi_i := s_i + n_i \in T_iu$, where $s_i \in S_iu$ and $n_i \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}u$.
	Then, since $p^k \in \mathcal{B}$, we have $\langle n_i, p^k - u\rangle \leq 0$, leading to
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_th1_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\langle G_iu + \xi_i, p^k - u\rangle &=& \langle G_iu + s_i, p^k - u\rangle + \langle n_i, p^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \langle G_iu + s_i, p^k - u_{[i]}^k\rangle + \langle G_iu + s_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Since $0 \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}(u)$ for all $u \in \mathcal{C}_i$, $i = 1, \cdots, n$, we have $\mathrm{gra}(G_i + S_i) \subseteq \mathrm{gra}(\Phi_i)$.
	Thus, the monotonicity of $\Phi_i$ immediately implies that $G_i + S_i$ is monotone.
	Moreover, since $u \in \mathcal{B} \subseteq \mathcal{C}_i$ and $n_i^k \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}u_{[i]}^k$, we have $\langle n_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \geq 0$.
	Using this relation and the monotonicity of $G_i + S_i$, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_th1_proof3}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle &=& \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + s_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle + \langle n_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + s_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \langle G_iu + s_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	From \eqref{eq:DFKM_th1_proof1}, since $\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert \to 0$ and $\mathbf{v}^k \to \mathbf{v}^{\star}$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.1em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k\Vert &=& \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k - \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert  
		\leq  \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert + \Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert \Vert\mathbf{v}^k\Vert < M_1 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k$ is the $i$-th block of $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k$, we also have 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\VertG_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert \leq \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k\Vert < M_1 < +\infty \qquad \forall i = 1, \cdots, n.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\mathcal{B}$ is compact, we have $D_{\mathcal{B}} := \sup_{u, u' \in \mathcal{B}} \Vertu - u'\Vert < +\infty$.
	Then, since $p^k, u \in \mathcal{B}$, we have $\Vertp^k - u\Vert \leq D_{\mathcal{B}}$.
	
	Using the continuity of $G_i$ (due to its Lipschitz continuity), the boundedness of $u \in \mathcal{B}$, and \eqref{eq:S_bounded}, there exists $M_2 > 0$ such that $\VertG_iu + s_i\Vert < M_2 < +\infty$.
	Substituting \eqref{eq:DFKM_th1_proof3} into \eqref{eq:DFKM_th1_proof2} and then using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the last relation, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\langle G_iu + \xi_i, p^k - u\rangle &\leq& \VertG_iu + s_i\Vert\Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert + \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& M_2\Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert  + \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Summing the last inequality from $i = 1$ to $n$, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_iu + \xi_i, p^k - u\rangle &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle + M_2 \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - p^k\rangle +  \langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k), p^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&& + {~} M_2 \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \VertG_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert \Vertu_{[i]}^k - p^k\Vert + \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert \Vertp^k - u\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&& + {~} M_2 \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& (M_1 + M_2) \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertu_{[i]}^k - p^k\Vert + D_{\mathcal{B}}\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& (M_1 + M_2) (n\kappa + \sqrt{n})\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k\Vert + D_{\mathcal{B}}\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Taking the supremum on both sides of the last inequality, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(p^k) &\leq& (M_1 + M_2) (n\kappa + \sqrt{n})\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k\Vert + D_{\mathcal{B}}\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this relation and the results in part (ii) and (iii), we conclude that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(p^k) = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k) = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	  
	                                             
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent$\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{The convergence of the FBS residual.}
	Let $\bar{G}u := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n G_iu$ and $\bar{T}u := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n T_iu$.
	Consider the FBS residual $\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}u := \frac{1}{\lambda}(u - J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(u - \lambda \bar{G}u))$.
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, it is obvious that $\bar{G}$ is also $L$-Lipschitz continuous and $\bar{\Phi} := \bar{G} + \bar{T}$ is maximally monotone.
	We consider two cases corresponding to  Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} and Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}.
	
	\noindent\textbf{Case $\mathrm{(i)}$.}
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, let $q^k := J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(\bar{u}^k - \lambda \bar{G}\bar{u}^k)$.
	Since $\{\bar{u}^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$ and $J_{\lambda \bar{T}}$ is continuous, we have $\lim_{k \to \infty} q^k = J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(u^{\star} - \lambda \bar{G}u^{\star})$.
	However, since $u^{\star}$ is a solution of \eqref{eq:DGE}, we also have $0 \in \bar{G}u^{\star} + \bar{T}u^{\star}$, meaning that $u^{\star}$ is also a solution of $0 \in \bar{G}u^{\star} + \bar{T}u^{\star}$.
	This implies $u^{\star} = J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(u^{\star} - \lambda \bar{G}u^{\star})$, and therefore, $\{q^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$.
	In particular, both $\{q^k\}$ and $\{\bar{u}^k - q^k\}$ are bounded, and thus there exists $M_1 > 0$ such that $\Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert \leq M_1$ and $\Vertq^k - u^{\star}\Vert \leq M_1$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	
	Next, by the definition of $q^k$, we have $\frac{1}{\lambda}(\bar{u}^k - q^k) - \bar{G}\bar{u}^k \in \bar{T}q^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n T_i q^k$.
	Thus, there exist $\zeta_i^k \in T_i q^k$ for all $i = 1, \cdots, n$, such that $\frac{1}{n} \sum_{i=1}^n \zeta_i^k = \frac{1}{\lambda}(\bar{u}^k - q^k) - \bar{G}\bar{u}^k$.
	Let $\breve{w}_i^k := G_i q^k + \zeta_i^k \in \Phi_i q^k$.
	Then, we have $\sum_{i=1}^n \breve{w}_i^k = \frac{n}{\lambda}(\bar{u}^k - q^k) + n(\bar{G}q^k - \bar{G}\bar{u}^k)$.
	Since $\{\bar{u}^k - q^k\}$ is bounded and $\bar{G}$ is Lipschitz continuous, we also have $\{\sum_{i=1}^n \breve{w}_i^k\}$ is bounded, i.e., there exists $M_2 > 0$ such that $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n \breve{w}_i^k\Vert \leq M_2$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	
	We next show that each $\{\breve{w}_i^k\}$ is bounded for all $i = 1, \cdots, n$.
	Recall the interior solution $u^{\dagger}$ from Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, there exists $\delta > 0$ such that $B_{\delta}(u^{\dagger}) \subset \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(\Phi_i))$, where $B_{\delta}(u^{\dagger}) = \{u \in \mathbb{R}^p: \Vertu - u^{\dagger}\Vert \leq \delta\}$.
	By Lemma~\ref{le:monotone_local_bounded}, there exists $M_3 > 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\hat{w}_i\Vert \leq M_3, \quad \hat{w}_i \in \Phi_iy, \quad y \in B_{\delta}(u^{\dagger}), \quad i = 1, \cdots, n.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	For $\breve{w}_i^k \neq 0$, let $e_i^k := \frac{\breve{w}_i^k}{\Vert\breve{w}_i^k\Vert}$, define $y_i^k := u^{\dagger} + \delta e_i^k \in B_{\delta}(u^{\dagger})$, and choose $\hat{w}_i^k \in \Phi_iy^k$.
	By the monotonicity of $\Phi_i$, we have $0 \leq \langle \breve{w}_i^k - \hat{w}_i^k, q^k - y_i^k\rangle = \langle \breve{w}_i^k - \hat{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger} - \delta e_i^k\rangle$.
	Expanding this expression with a notice that $\langle \breve{w}_i^k, e_i^k\rangle = \Vert\breve{w}_i^k\Vert$ and then rearranging the result, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\delta \Vert\breve{w}_i^k\Vert &\leq& \langle \breve{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger}\rangle - \langle \hat{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger}\rangle + \delta\langle \hat{w}_i^k, e_i^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \langle \breve{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger}\rangle + \Vert\hat{w}_i^k\Vert \Vertq^k - u^{\dagger}\Vert + \delta\Vert\hat{w}_i^k\Vert\Verte_i^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \langle \breve{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger}\rangle + M_3\Vertq^k - u^{\dagger}\Vert + \delta M_3.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Summing the last inequality from $i=1$ to $n$, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\delta \sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\breve{w}_i^k\Vert &\leq& \langle \sum_{i=1}^n \breve{w}_i^k, q^k - u^{\dagger}\rangle + nM_3\Vertq^k - u^{\dagger}\Vert + n\delta M_3 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n \breve{w}_i^k\Vert(\Vertq^k - u^{\star}\Vert + \Vertu^{\star} - u^{\dagger}\Vert) + nM_3(\Vertq^k - u^{\star}\Vert + \Vertu^{\star} - u^{\dagger}\Vert) + n\delta M_3 \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& (M_2 + nM_3)(M_1 + \Vertu^{\star} - u^{\dagger}\Vert) + n\delta M_3.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining the boundedness of $\{G_i u_{[i]}^k+\xi_i^k\}$ established in part (iv) with the preceding bound on $\sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\breve{w}_i^k\Vert = \sum_{i=1}^n \VertG_i q^k + \zeta_i^k\Vert$, there exists $M_4 > 0$ such that $\sum_{i=1}^n \big(\VertG_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i q^k + \zeta_i^k\Vert\big) \leq M_4$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	
	Now, for $\xi_i^k \in T_iu_{[i]}^k$, by the monotonicity of $G_i + T_i$, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		0 &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k) - (G_i q^k + \zeta_i^k), u_{[i]}^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle (G_i u_i^k + \xi_i^k) - (G_i q^k + \zeta_i^k), (u_i^k - \bar{u}^k) + (\bar{u}^k - q^k)\rangle.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Partially expanding this inner product and then using $\sum_{i=1}^n \zeta_i^k = \frac{n}{\lambda}(\bar{u}^k - q^k) - n\bar{G}\bar{u}^k$, we get
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{rcl}	
			\frac{n}{\lambda} \Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \left\langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k), \bar{u}^k - q^k\right\rangle + n \langle \bar{G}\bar{u}^k - \bar{G}q^k, \bar{u}^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
			&& + {~} \sum_{i=1}^n \left\langle (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k) - (G_i q^k + \zeta_i^k), u_i^k - \bar{u}^k\right\rangle.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	From the $L$-Lipschitz continuity of $G_i$, we have 
	$n \langle \bar{G}\bar{u}^k - \bar{G}q^k, \bar{u}^k - q^k\rangle = \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i \bar{u}^k - G_i q^k, \bar{u}^k - q^k\rangle \leq n L\Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2$.
	Substituting this bound into the last inequality, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		n(\frac{1}{\lambda} - L) \Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2 &\leq& \langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k), \bar{u}^k - q^k\rangle +  \sum_{i=1}^n \langle (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k) - (G_i q^k + \zeta_i^k), u_i^k - \bar{u}^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		
		&\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert \Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert +  \sum_{i=1}^n \big(\VertG_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i q^k + \zeta_i^k\Vert\big) \Vertu_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		
		&\leq& M_1 \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert + M_4 \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertu_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this inequality with the results of part $\mathrm{(ii)}$ and $\mathrm{(iii)}$, we conclude that
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{rcl}	
			\Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, since $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vert\bar{u}^k - J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(\bar{u}^k - \lambda \bar{G}\bar{u}^k)\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert^2$, we also get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	           
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent
	\textbf{Case $\mathrm{(ii)}$.}
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}, let $q^k := J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(p^k - \lambda \bar{G}p^k)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$ as defined in part $\mathrm{(iv)}$.
	Then, there exist $\zeta_i^k \in T_i q^k$ such that $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n \zeta_i^k = \frac{1}{\lambda}(p^k - q^k) - \bar{G}p^k$.
	Using the definition of the restricted gap function, the last expression, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the $L$-Lipschitz continuity of $\bar{G}$, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(p^k) &\geq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_iq^k + \zeta_i^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& n\langle \bar{G}q^k + \frac{1}{\lambda}(p^k - q^k) - \bar{G}p^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \frac{n}{\lambda}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 - n\langle \bar{G}p^k - \bar{G}q^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq&  \frac{n}{\lambda}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 - n\Vert\bar{G}p^k - \bar{G}q^k\Vert\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq&  n\big(\frac{1}{\lambda} - L\big)\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this with the results in part (iv), we conclude that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, since $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vertp^k - J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(p^k - \lambda \bar{G}p^k)\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2$, we also get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
      
     
                                              
\vspace{0.75ex}
\noindent$\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{The convergence of operator residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert$ when $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$.}
Using the triangle inequality and the $L$-Lipschitz continuity of $G_i$, we have
\begin{equation*}
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\begin{array}{rcl}	
	\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert &=& \left\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k - \sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k + \sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k \right\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
	&\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \VertG_i \bar{u}^k - G_i u_{[i]}^k\Vert + \left\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k\right\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
	&\leq& L\sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert + \left\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k\right\Vert.
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
From the results of parts (b) and (c), the last inequality implies that
\begin{equation*}
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\begin{array}{rcl}	
	\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big),
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- head-17: body_md -->
~
	Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap},
	let us denote by $\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) := \sup_{u \in {\mathcal{B}}} \mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k)$, 
	where $\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) := \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u\rangle$. 
	By inserting $u_{[i]}^k$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
	\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) = \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $G_i + T_i$ is monotone, for ${\xi}_i^k \in T_i {u}_i^k$, we have $\langle G_i u + \xi_i, {u}_i^k - u\rangle \leq \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - u\rangle$.
	Substituting this relation into the last inequality, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{G}(u, \bar{u}^k) &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_i^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, {u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k, \bar{u}^k - u\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_i u + \xi_i, \bar{u}^k - u_i^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \left\langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k), \bar{u}^k - u\right\rangle + \sum_{i=1}^n \left\langle (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k) - (G_i u + \xi_i), {u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\right\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n \left( \VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \right) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Taking supremum on both sides of this inequality over ${\mathcal{B}}$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k) &\leq& \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert \sup_{u \in B} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&& + {~} \sum_{i=1}^n \sup_{u \in B} \left( \VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert + \VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \right) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\{u_i^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$ for all $i \in [n]$, $\bar{u}^k = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n u_i^k$ converges to $u^{\star}$, and thus there exists $M_1 > 0$ such that $\sup_{u \in {\mathcal{B}}} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u\Vert \leq M_1$.
	Next, we have $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k = (\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k) - \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$, where the first term $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$ converges to zero as proved in part (b) and the second term $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k$ converges to $\mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^{\star}$, leading to the boundedness of $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k$.
	Thus, there exists $M_2 > 0$ such that $\VertG_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k\Vert \leq M_2$ for all $i \in [n]$.
	Finally, since ${\mathcal{B}} \subset \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T_i))$, by Lemma~\ref{le:monotone_local_bounded}, there exists $M_3 > 0$ such that $\VertG_i u + \xi_i\Vert \leq M_3$ for all $i = 1, \cdots, n$.
	Substituting these bounds into the last inequality, we get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) &\leq& M_1 \Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i {u}_i^k + {\xi}_i^k)\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n (M_2 + M_3) \Vert{u}_i^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this relation and the results in part (ii) and (iii), we conclude that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k) = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k) = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-17 -->
<!-- head-18: body_md -->
Under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}, we have $\mathrm{dom}(T_i) = \mathcal{C}_i$, and thus $\mathcal{B} = \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathcal{C}_i$ is nonempty, compact, and convex, and $\bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathcal{C}_i\right) \ne \emptyset$.
	By \cite[Corollary 3]{bauschke1999strong}, the collection $\{\mathcal{C}_1, \cdots, \mathcal{C}_n\}$ is boundedly linear regular, i.e., for every bounded set $\mathcal{Q} \subseteq \mathbb{R}^p$, there exists $\kappa_{\mathcal{Q}} > 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
		\mathrm{dist}\left(u, \mathcal{B}\right) \leq \kappa_{\mathcal{Q}} \max_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(u, \mathcal{C}_i\right), \qquad u \in \mathcal{Q}.
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\bar{u}^k \to u^{\star}$, $\{\bar{u}^k\}_{k \geq 0}$ is bounded.
	Taking $\mathcal{Q} := \{\bar{u}^k\}_{k \geq 0}$.
	Then, there exists $\kappa > 0$ such that 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{B}\right) \leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{C}_i\right), \qquad \forall k \geq 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Next, let $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$, then we have $\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert = \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{B}\right)$.
	Since $u_{[i]}^k \in \mathrm{dom}(T_i) = \mathcal{C}_i$, we obtain from the last inequality that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert \leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \mathrm{dist}\left(\bar{u}^k, \mathcal{C}_i\right) 
		\leq \kappa \max\limits_{i=1, \cdots, n} \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert 
		= \kappa \Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert_{\infty} \leq \kappa \Vert\Pi\mathbf{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using this relation, triangle inequality, and Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we can derive that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\sum_{i=1}^n \Vertp^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert &\leq& \sum_{i=1}^n \big(\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert\big) \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& n\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& n\Vertp^k - \bar{u}^k\Vert + \sqrt{n}\big(\sum_{i=1}^n \Vert\bar{u}^k - u_{[i]}^k\Vert^2\big)^{1/2} \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& (n\kappa + \sqrt{n})\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Now, fix arbitrary $u \in \mathcal{B}$ and arbitrary $\xi_i := s_i + n_i \in T_iu$, where $s_i \in S_iu$ and $n_i \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}u$.
	Then, since $p^k \in \mathcal{B}$, we have $\langle n_i, p^k - u\rangle \leq 0$, leading to
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_th1_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\langle G_iu + \xi_i, p^k - u\rangle &=& \langle G_iu + s_i, p^k - u\rangle + \langle n_i, p^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\leq& \langle G_iu + s_i, p^k - u_{[i]}^k\rangle + \langle G_iu + s_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Since $0 \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}(u)$ for all $u \in \mathcal{C}_i$, $i = 1, \cdots, n$, we have $\mathrm{gra}(G_i + S_i) \subseteq \mathrm{gra}(\Phi_i)$.
	Thus, the monotonicity of $\Phi_i$ immediately implies that $G_i + S_i$ is monotone.
	Moreover, since $u \in \mathcal{B} \subseteq \mathcal{C}_i$ and $n_i^k \in \mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{C}_i}u_{[i]}^k$, we have $\langle n_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \geq 0$.
	Using this relation and the monotonicity of $G_i + S_i$, we have
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_th1_proof3}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle &=& \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + s_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle + \langle n_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \langle G_i u_{[i]}^k + s_i^k, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq& \langle G_iu + s_i, u_{[i]}^k - u\rangle.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	From \eqref{eq:DFKM_th1_proof1}, since $\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert \to 0$ and $\mathbf{v}^k \to \mathbf{v}^{\star}$, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.1em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k\Vert &=& \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k - \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert  
		\leq  \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{K}^\top \mathbf{v}^k\Vert + \Vert\mathbf{K}\Vert \Vert\mathbf{v}^k\Vert < M_1 < +\infty.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k$ is the $i$-th block of $\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k$, we also have 
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}	
		\VertG_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert \leq \Vert\mathbf{G}\mathbf{u}^k + \boldsymbol{\xi}^k\Vert < M_1 < +\infty \qquad \forall i = 1, \cdots, n.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\mathcal{B}$ is compact, we have $D_{\mathcal{B}} := \sup_{u, u' \in \mathcal{B}} \Vertu - u'\Vert < +\infty$.
	Then, since $p^k, u \in \mathcal{B}$, we have $\Vertp^k - u\Vert \leq D_{\mathcal{B}}$.
<!-- end head-18 -->
<!-- head-19: body_md -->
Under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, let $q^k := J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(\bar{u}^k - \lambda \bar{G}\bar{u}^k)$.
	Since $\{\bar{u}^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$ and $J_{\lambda \bar{T}}$ is continuous, we have $\lim_{k \to \infty} q^k = J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(u^{\star} - \lambda \bar{G}u^{\star})$.
	However, since $u^{\star}$ is a solution of \eqref{eq:DGE}, we also have $0 \in \bar{G}u^{\star} + \bar{T}u^{\star}$, meaning that $u^{\star}$ is also a solution of $0 \in \bar{G}u^{\star} + \bar{T}u^{\star}$.
	This implies $u^{\star} = J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(u^{\star} - \lambda \bar{G}u^{\star})$, and therefore, $\{q^k\}$ converges to $u^{\star}$.
	In particular, both $\{q^k\}$ and $\{\bar{u}^k - q^k\}$ are bounded, and thus there exists $M_1 > 0$ such that $\Vert\bar{u}^k - q^k\Vert \leq M_1$ and $\Vertq^k - u^{\star}\Vert \leq M_1$ for all $k \geq 0$.
<!-- end head-19 -->
<!-- head-20: body_md -->
Under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}, let $q^k := J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(p^k - \lambda \bar{G}p^k)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$ as defined in part $\mathrm{(iv)}$.
	Then, there exist $\zeta_i^k \in T_i q^k$ such that $\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n \zeta_i^k = \frac{1}{\lambda}(p^k - q^k) - \bar{G}p^k$.
	Using the definition of the restricted gap function, the last expression, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and the $L$-Lipschitz continuity of $\bar{G}$, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\mathrm{Gap}_{\mathcal{B}}(p^k) &\geq& \sum_{i=1}^n \langle G_iq^k + \zeta_i^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& n\langle \bar{G}q^k + \frac{1}{\lambda}(p^k - q^k) - \bar{G}p^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&=& \frac{n}{\lambda}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 - n\langle \bar{G}p^k - \bar{G}q^k, p^k - q^k\rangle \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq&  \frac{n}{\lambda}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 - n\Vert\bar{G}p^k - \bar{G}q^k\Vert\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert \vspace{1ex}\\
		&\geq&  n\big(\frac{1}{\lambda} - L\big)\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Combining this with the results in part (iv), we conclude that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Finally, since $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vertp^k - J_{\lambda \bar{T}}(p^k - \lambda \bar{G}p^k)\Vert^2 = \frac{1}{\lambda^2}\Vertp^k - q^k\Vert^2$, we also get
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad \text{and} \qquad \Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2 = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-20 -->
<!-- head-21: body_md -->
\label{subsubsec:ND_case2}
\vspace{-1.5ex}
Now, we design a distributed FFP method to solve  \eqref{eq:DGE} under the following assumption.

                   
\begin{assumption}\label{as:DGE_cocoercive}
	The distributed composite inclusion \eqref{eq:DGE} satisfies the following conditions:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] (\textbf{Local co-coercivity}) $G_i$ is $\frac{1}{L}$-co-coercive, for all $i \in [n]$.
		\item[(ii)] (\textbf{Local maximal monotonicity}) $T_i$ is maximally monotone for all $i \in [n]$.
	\end{compactitem}
\end{assumption}
   
First, we establish the following lemma to transform \eqref{eq:DGE} to \eqref{eq:precond_GE}.
The proof of this lemma is given in Appendix~\ref{apdx:le:DGE_cocoercive}.
              
\begin{lemma}\label{le:DGE_cocoercive}
	Let $G$ and $T$ in \eqref{eq:DGE} satisfy  Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_cocoercive} and $\tau \sigma \Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert < 2$. 
	Then
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] 
		The operator $\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{G}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is $\frac{1}{\tilde{L}}$-co-coercive w.r.t. $\Vert\cdot\Vert_{\mathbb{P}}$, where $\tilde{L} := \frac{2\tau L}{2 - \tau \sigma \Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert}$.
		
		\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] 
		The operator $\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{T}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is maximally monotone w.r.t. $\langle \cdot, \cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{P}}$.
	\end{compactitem}
\end{lemma}
   
Next, utilizing the parameters from Corollary~\ref{co:FKM_scheme2+_convergence}, the scheme \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme} reduces to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_scheme2}
\tag{ND-DFFP2}
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\left\{
\begin{array}{lcl}	
	\mathbf{d}^k &:=& \frac{1}{2}(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - W\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k), \vspace{1ex}\\
	\mathbf{u}^{k+1} &:=& J_{\tau \mathbf{T}} \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \tau \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tau \mathbf{G}\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k  -  2 \tau \sigma \mathbf{d}^k\right), \vspace{1ex}\\
	\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \mathbf{u}^{k+1}, \vspace{1ex}\\
	\hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k+1} &:=& \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k + \theta_k \left(\hat{\mathbf{u}}^k - \hat{\mathbf{u}}^{k-1} \right) - \hat{\eta}_k\hat{\mathbf{w}}^{k+1} + \hat{\gamma}_k \hat{\mathbf{w}}^k , \vspace{1ex}\\
	\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k+1} &:=& \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k + \theta_k \left(\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k - \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{k-1} \right) + \sigma (\hat{\eta}_k\mathbf{d}^k - \hat{\gamma}_k \mathbf{d}^{k-1}),
\end{array}
\right.
\end{equation}
where the iterates are initialized as $\hat{\mathbf{u}}^{-1} = \hat{\mathbf{u}}^0 = \mathbf{u}^0$, $\tilde{\mathbf{v}}^{-1} = \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^0 = 0$, $\hat{\mathbf{w}}^0 = 0$, and $\mathbf{d}^{-1} = 0$, and the parameters are updated according to \eqref{eq:DFKM2_params}.
   
Then, the convergence of \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme2} can be stated in the following theorem.
                
\begin{theorem}[\textbf{Co-coercive setting}]\label{th:DFKM_convergence_cocoercive}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_cocoercive} holds. 
	Let $\{(\mathbf{u}^k, \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme2} using $t_0$ given in \eqref{eq:t0_cond2} and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM2_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-2ex}
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 1, \qquad \nu \in (0, r-1), \qquad 			
			\tau L + \tau \sigma (1 - \lambda_{\min}(W)) < 2, \vspace{1ex}\\
			t_{k} := k + t_0, \quad \theta_k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_{k+1}}, \qquad  \hat{\eta}_k = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu (t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}}, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \hat{\gamma}_k = \theta_k.
		\end{array}
		\hspace{-2ex}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{$($Local iterates$)$}]
	The local iterates $\{u_{[i]}^k\}$ and $\{\hat{u}_{[i]}^k\}$ converge to the same $u^\star \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$ of the original \eqref{eq:DGE} problem for all nodes $i \in [n]$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{$($Consensus errors$)$}]
	The consensus errors $\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k \Vert$ and $\Vert\Pi \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k \Vert$ converge to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{$($Aggregated residual$)$}]
	The aggregated residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\xi_i^k \in Tu_{[i]}^k$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{$($Restricted gap$)$}]
	If, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then the restricted gap value $\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\bar{u}^k := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n u_{[i]}^k$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{$($FBS residual$)$}]
	For a given $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{L})$, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~ \ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{$($Special case$)$}]
	If $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$ $($i.e., \eqref{eq:DGE} reduces to \eqref{eq:DNE}$)$, then $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	\end{compactitem}
\end{theorem}
                   

                         
\begin{proof}
	The proof of this theorem is similar to Theorem~\ref{th:DFKM_convergence}, but using Corollary~\ref{co:FKM_scheme2+_convergence} (or Theorem~\ref{th:FKM2_convergence}).
	Thus, we omit the details here.
\end{proof}
                 

                                                                
                                                                                                       
                                                                
\vspace{-1.25ex}
<!-- end head-21 -->
<!-- assumption-6: body_md -->
\label{as:DGE_cocoercive}
	The distributed composite inclusion \eqref{eq:DGE} satisfies the following conditions:
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[(i)] (\textbf{Local co-coercivity}) $G_i$ is $\frac{1}{L}$-co-coercive, for all $i \in [n]$.
		\item[(ii)] (\textbf{Local maximal monotonicity}) $T_i$ is maximally monotone for all $i \in [n]$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end assumption-6 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{le:DGE_cocoercive}
	Let $G$ and $T$ in \eqref{eq:DGE} satisfy  Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_cocoercive} and $\tau \sigma \Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert < 2$. 
	Then
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] 
		The operator $\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{G}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is $\frac{1}{\tilde{L}}$-co-coercive w.r.t. $\Vert\cdot\Vert_{\mathbb{P}}$, where $\tilde{L} := \frac{2\tau L}{2 - \tau \sigma \Vert\mathbb{I} - W\Vert}$.
		
		\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] 
		The operator $\mathbb{P}^{-1} \mathbb{T}$ in \eqref{eq:precond_GE} is maximally monotone w.r.t. $\langle \cdot, \cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{P}}$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{th:DFKM_convergence_cocoercive}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that Assumption~\ref{as:DGE_cocoercive} holds. 
	Let $\{(\mathbf{u}^k, \tilde{\mathbf{v}}^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_scheme2} using $t_0$ given in \eqref{eq:t0_cond2} and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM2_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-2ex}
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 1, \qquad \nu \in (0, r-1), \qquad 			
			\tau L + \tau \sigma (1 - \lambda_{\min}(W)) < 2, \vspace{1ex}\\
			t_{k} := k + t_0, \quad \theta_k := \frac{t_k - r}{t_{k+1}}, \qquad  \hat{\eta}_k = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}} + \frac{\nu (t_k - 1)}{t_k t_{k+1}}, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \hat{\gamma}_k = \theta_k.
		\end{array}
		\hspace{-2ex}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{$($Local iterates$)$}]
	The local iterates $\{u_{[i]}^k\}$ and $\{\hat{u}_{[i]}^k\}$ converge to the same $u^\star \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$ of the original \eqref{eq:DGE} problem for all nodes $i \in [n]$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{$($Consensus errors$)$}]
	The consensus errors $\Vert\Pi \mathbf{u}^k \Vert$ and $\Vert\Pi \hat{\mathbf{u}}^k \Vert$ converge to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{$($Aggregated residual$)$}]
	The aggregated residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i u_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\xi_i^k \in Tu_{[i]}^k$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{$($Restricted gap$)$}]
	If, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then the restricted gap value $\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{u}^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\bar{u}^k := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n u_{[i]}^k$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{$($FBS residual$)$}]
	For a given $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{L})$, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{u}^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~ \ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{u}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{$($Special case$)$}]
	If $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$ $($i.e., \eqref{eq:DGE} reduces to \eqref{eq:DNE}$)$, then $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{u}^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The proof of this theorem is similar to Theorem~\ref{th:DFKM_convergence}, but using Corollary~\ref{co:FKM_scheme2+_convergence} (or Theorem~\ref{th:FKM2_convergence}).
	Thus, we omit the details here.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{le:DFKM_3op_reform}
	Let $\gamma_1, \cdots, \gamma_n > 0$, $\mathbf{\Gamma} := \mathrm{diag}\left(\gamma_1, \cdots, \gamma_n\right)$, and $\mathbf{u} = (u_{[1]}, \cdots, u_{[n]}) \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$, where $u_{[i]} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ is the local variable of agent $i \in [n]$.
	Define $T: \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$ and $G: \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \to \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$ as in \eqref{eq:GE_notation}.
	Let $\mathbf{K} := \left(\frac{\mathbb{I} - \mathbf{W}}{2}\right)^{1/2} \succeq 0$ and $\mathbf{M} := (\kappa \mathbb{I} - \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma}^2 \mathbf{K})^{1/2} \succ 0$, where $\kappa > \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{K}^2 \mathbf{\Gamma}\Vert$.
	Let ${A}, {C}: \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$ and ${B}: \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \to \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_3op_operators}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		{A}(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) & := & \left(\mathbf{\Gamma} T(\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}), \ \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\breve{\mathbf{a}}) \right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		{B}(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) & := & \left(\mathbf{\Gamma} G (\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}), \ 0 \right), \vspace{1ex}\\
		{C}(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) & := & \left(\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{K} \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a} + \mathbf{M}\breve{\mathbf{a}}), \ 
		\mathbf{M}\mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a} + \mathbf{M}\breve{\mathbf{a}}) \right).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item[$\mathrm{(i)}$] 
		The point $(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) \in \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$ satisfies
		\begin{equation}\label{eq:le_DFKM_3op_reform1}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			0 \in ({A} + {B} + {C})(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) \subset \mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
		\end{array}
		\end{equation}
		if and only if $\mathbf{a} = \mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}(u, \cdots, u)$ for some $u \in \mathbb{R}^p$ and $u$ solves $0 \in \sum_{i=1}^n\big(G_iu + T_iu\big)$.
		
		\item[$\mathrm{(ii)}$] 
		Suppose $T_i$ for all $i \in [n]$ are maximally monotone, and $G_i$ are monotone and $L_i$-Lipschitz continuous for all $i \in [n]$.
		Then, ${A}$ and ${C}$ are maximally monotone, and ${B}$ is monotone and $L_B$-Lipschitz continuous with $L_B = \max_{ i \in [n]} \{\gamma_i^2 L_i\}$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- head-22: body_md -->
Let $u = (\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}})$, $\hat{u} = (\hat{\mathbf{a}}, \hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}})$, $v = (\mathbf{b}, \breve{\mathbf{b}})$, 
	$\hat{v} = (\hat{\mathbf{b}}, \hat{\breve{\mathbf{b}}})$, $z_u = (z_{\mathbf{a}}, z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}})$, 
	and $z_v = (z_{\mathbf{b}}, z_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}})$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$. 
	We update
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcllcl}
		\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k &=& \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} \mathbf{a}^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_{\mathbf{a}}^k, 
		\qquad \text{and} \qquad
		\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k &=& \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} \breve{\mathbf{a}}^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\hat{\mathbf{b}}^k &=& \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} \mathbf{b}^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_{\mathbf{b}}^k, 
		\qquad \text{and} \qquad
		\hat{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k &=& \frac{t_k - r}{t_k} \breve{\mathbf{b}}^k + \frac{r}{t_k} z_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-22 -->
<!-- head-23: body_md -->
Let us denote by $y_u = (y_{\mathbf{a}}, y_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}})$, $\tilde{w}_u = (\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}, \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}})$, and $\tilde{w}_v = (\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}, \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}})$ in $\mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$.
	We update
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		y_{\mathbf{a}}^k = \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \frac{\gamma_k - \beta_k}{1 - \tau \sigma} (\tau \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^k + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^k)
		\qquad \text{and} \qquad 
		y_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = \hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \frac{\gamma_k - \beta_k}{1 - \tau \sigma} (\tau \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-23 -->
<!-- head-24: body_md -->
By the definition of ${A}(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) = (\mathbf{\Gamma} T(\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}), \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\breve{\mathbf{a}}))$, we have $J_{\tau A} = (J_{\tau \mathbf{\Gamma} T \mathbf{\Gamma}}, 0)$. 
	Thus, using ${B}(\mathbf{a}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}) = (\mathbf{\Gamma} G (\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}), 0)$, we update
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{a}^{k+1} = J_{\tau \mathbf{\Gamma} T \mathbf{\Gamma}} (\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \tau \hat{\mathbf{b}}^k - \tau \mathbf{\Gamma} G (\mathbf{\Gamma} y_{\mathbf{a}}^k) + \tau \beta_k \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^k)
		\qquad \text{and} \qquad
		\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} = 0.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	By induction, we can prove that $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k = 0$, $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$ (and $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$ in the later step).
	This proof is nontrivial and can be found in Appendix~\ref{le:NI_DFFP_derivation3}.
<!-- end head-24 -->
<!-- head-25: body_md -->
Let $\tilde{v} = (\tilde{\mathbf{b}}, \tilde{\breve{\mathbf{b}}})$ and $v = (\mathbf{b}, \breve{\mathbf{b}})$  in $\mathbb{R}^{n\times p} \times \mathbb{R}^{n\times p}$.
	First, we update 
\begin{equation*}
\arraycolsep=0.2em
\begin{array}{lcl}
	\tilde{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1} = \hat{\mathbf{b}}^k + \sigma(2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k) + \sigma\beta_k \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^k
	\ \  \text{and} \ \
	\tilde{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^{k+1} = \hat{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k + \sigma(2\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} - \hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k) + \sigma\beta_k \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k. 
\end{array}
\end{equation*}
	Next, to obtain the update of $v^{k+1}$, we need an explicit formula for $J_{\sigma^{-1} C}$.
	Let $\mathbf{E} = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma} & \mathbf{M}
	\end{bmatrix}$, then for $\mathbf{v} = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{b} \\ \breve{\mathbf{b}}
	\end{bmatrix}$, we can write $C(\mathbf{v}) = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\mathbf{E}\mathbf{v})$.
	By definition, $\mathbf{r} = J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(\mathbf{t})$ if and only if $\mathbf{t} \in \mathbf{r} + \sigma^{-1}C(\mathbf{r})$, or equivalently, $\mathbf{t} \in \mathbf{r} + \mathbf{E}^\top \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}} (\mathbf{E}\mathbf{r})$.
	Thus, we must have $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{r} = 0$, and there exists $\mathbf{s} \in \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}} (\mathbf{E}\mathbf{r})$ such that $\mathbf{t} = \mathbf{r} + \mathbf{E}^\top\mathbf{s}$.
	Multiplying this by $\mathbf{E}$ and using $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{r} = 0$ and $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^\top = \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma}^2\mathbf{K} + \mathbf{M}^2 = \kappa \mathbb{I}$, we obtain $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{t} = \kappa \mathbf{s}$, or $\mathbf{s} = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{t}$.
	Therefore, we can show that
	\begin{equation*}
		J_{\sigma^{-1}C}(\mathbf{t}) = \mathbf{r} = \mathbf{t} - \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{s} = \mathbf{t} - \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\mathbf{t} = (\mathbb{I} - \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E})\mathbf{t}.
	\end{equation*}
	Using this expression, we obtain
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^{k+1} &=& \tilde{v}^{k+1} - \sigma \left( \frac{1}{\sigma}\tilde{v}^{k+1} - \frac{\kappa^{-1}}{\sigma} \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1} \right) = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E} \tilde{v}^{k+1},
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where we have the following expression from the fifth line of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1} = \mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k + \sigma \mathbf{E}(2u^{k+1} - \hat{u}^k) + \sigma \beta_k \mathbf{E}\tilde{w}_v^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Let us break this expression down.
	First, since $u^{k+1} = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{a}^{k+1} \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix}$ and $\hat{u}^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix}$, we have 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof3}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{E}(2u^{k+1} - \hat{u}^k) = \mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Next, from the fifth line of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, we get
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\frac{1}{\sigma}(\tilde{v}^{k} - v^k) = \frac{1}{\sigma}(\hat{v}^{k-1} - v^k) + 2u^{k} - \hat{u}^{k-1} + \beta_{k-1} \tilde{w}_v^{k-1},
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	From the eighth line of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, we have $\tilde{w}_v^k = -(\hat{u}^{k-1} - u^k) + \frac{1}{\sigma}(\hat{v}^{k-1} - v^k) + \beta_{k-1} \tilde{w}_v^{k-1}$.
	Taking the difference of the last two relations and rearranging the result, we obtain
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\frac{1}{\sigma}(\tilde{v}^{k} - v^k) = \tilde{w}_v^k + u^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $v^k = J_{\sigma C^{-1}}(\tilde{v}^k)$, we have $\frac{1}{\sigma}(\tilde{v}^k - v^k) \in C^{-1}(v^k)$, which implies that
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^k \in C\left(\frac{1}{\sigma}(\tilde{v}^k - v^k)\right) = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}} (\mathbf{E}(\tilde{w}_v^k + u^k)).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, we must have $\mathbf{E}(\tilde{w}_v^k + u^k) = 0$, which implies that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof4}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{E}\tilde{w}_v^k = - \mathbf{E}u^k = -\begin{bmatrix}
			\mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma} & \mathbf{M}
		\end{bmatrix} \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{a}^k \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} = - \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}^k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Substituting \eqref{eq:DFKM_ni_proof3} and \eqref{eq:DFKM_ni_proof4} into \eqref{eq:DFKM_ni_proof2}, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof5}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1}  &=& \mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k + \sigma \mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \beta_k \mathbf{a}^k).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Substituting this into \eqref{eq:DFKM_ni_proof1}, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_ni_proof6}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^{k+1} &=& \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top\mathbf{E} \hat{v}^k + \kappa^{-1}\sigma \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \beta_k \mathbf{a}^k).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Now, substituting $\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top\mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k = \hat{v}^k$ from Lemma~\ref{le:NI_DFFP_derivation2} into \eqref{eq:DFKM_ni_proof6}, we get
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		v^{k+1} &=& \hat{v}^k + \kappa^{-1}\sigma \mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \beta_k \mathbf{a}^k).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	This expression can be explicitly rewritten as
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathbf{b}^{k+1} &=& \hat{\mathbf{b}}^k + \kappa^{-1}\sigma \mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{K}^2 \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \beta_k \mathbf{a}^k), \vspace{1ex}\\
		\breve{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1} &=& \mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@style\hat{\mathbf{b}}$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax^k + \kappa^{-1}\sigma \mathbf{M} \mathbf{K} \mathbf{\Gamma} (2\mathbf{a}^{k+1} - \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \beta_k \mathbf{a}^k)
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-25 -->
<!-- head-26: body_md -->
We update
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\left\{
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} = \frac{1}{\tau}(\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \mathbf{a}^{k+1}) - (\hat{\mathbf{b}}^k - \mathbf{b}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = \frac{1}{\tau}(\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1}) - (\hat{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k - \breve{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1} = -(\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k - \mathbf{a}^{k+1}) + \frac{1}{\sigma}(\hat{\mathbf{b}}^k - \mathbf{b}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^k, \vspace{1ex}\\
		\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^{k+1} = -(\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1}) + \frac{1}{\sigma}(\hat{\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@style\bf{b}$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax}^k - \breve{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k.
	\end{array}
	\right.
	\end{equation*}
<!-- end head-26 -->
<!-- head-27: body_md -->
We update
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		z_{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} = z_{\mathbf{a}}^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1-\tau\sigma)}(\tau\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} + \tau\sigma\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1})
		\ \ \text{and} \ \
		z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1-\tau\sigma)}(\tau\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} + \tau\sigma\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^{k+1}), \vspace{1ex}\\ 
		z_{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1} = z_{\mathbf{b}}^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1-\tau\sigma)}(\tau\sigma\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} + \sigma\tilde{w}_{\mathbf{b}}^{k+1})
		\ \ \text{and} \ \
		z_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^{k+1} = z_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1-\tau\sigma)}(\tau\sigma\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} + \sigma\tilde{w}_{\breve{\mathbf{b}}}^{k+1}).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}	
\end{compactitem}
Putting everything together, we obtain the detailed scheme \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme_init} in Appendix~\ref{apdx:DFKM_NI_scheme_init}.
Next, we also use appropriate linear variable transformations and the facts that $\mathbf{\Gamma}^2 = \mathbf{\Lambda} = \mathrm{diag}\left(\alpha_1, \cdots, \alpha_n\right)$ and $\mathbf{K} = \left(\frac{\mathbb{I} -  W}{2}\right)^{1/2}$, to obtain a simplified scheme \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme} in Appendix~\ref{apdx:DFKM_NI_scheme_init}.
<!-- end head-27 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{th:NI_DFKM_convergence}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that $G_i$ is monotone and $L_i$-Lipschitz continuous, and $T_i$ is maximally monotone for $i \in [n]$. 
	Let $\{\mathbf{x}^k\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme} using $t_0$ given in \eqref{eq:FKM_key_est2_t0} and
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:NI_DFKM_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\hspace{-2ex}
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 2, \quad \delta \in (0, r - 2), \quad \omega := r^2 - 3r + 3, \quad 0 < \nu < r - 2 - \delta, \vspace{1ex}\\
			0 < \alpha_i \leq \frac{1 - \tau \sigma}{2 \sqrt{1+\omega} \tau L_i}, \quad 2\kappa > \Vert\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}(\mathbb{I} -  \mathbf{W})\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}\Vert,  \vspace{1ex}\\
			t_{k} := k +  t_0, \quad \beta_k := \frac{\delta(t_k - r)}{2(r-1)t_k}, \quad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k}, \quad \text{and} \quad \gamma_k := 1,
		\end{array}
		\hspace{-2ex}
	\end{equation}
	where $\mathbf{\Lambda} = \mathrm{diag}\left(\alpha_1, \cdots, \alpha_n\right)$.
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactitem}
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{$($Local iterates$)$}]
	The local iterates $\{x_{[i]}^k\}$ converge to the same $u^\star \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$ of the original \eqref{eq:DGE} problem for all nodes $i \in [n]$.
	
 	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{$($Consensus error$)$}]
	The consensus error $\Vert\Pi \mathbf{x}^k \Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{$($Aggregated residual$)$}]
	The aggregated residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i x_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\xi_i^k \in Tx_{[i]}^k$.
		
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{$($Restricted gap$)$}]
	If, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then the restricted gap value $\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{x}^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\bar{x}^k := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n x_{[i]}^k$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~ \ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{x}^k)$.
	
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{$($FBS residual$)$}]
	For a given $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{\bar{L}})$ with $\bar{L} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n L_i$, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{x}^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	  
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{x}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{$($Special case$)$}]
	If $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$ $($i.e., \eqref{eq:DGE} reduces to \eqref{eq:DNE}$)$, then $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{x}^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	\end{compactitem}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
For the sake of clarity, we divide this proof into the following steps.
	
	                                             
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent\textbf{(i)~The convergence of iterate sequences.}
	Denote $\mathbf{\Gamma} = \mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2} \succ 0$, $\mathbf{K} = (\frac{\mathbb{I} - W}{2})^{1/2} \succeq 0$, 
	and $\mathbf{M} = (\kappa\mathbb{I} - \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma}^2 \mathbf{K})^{1/2} \succ 0$.
	From Lemma~\ref{le:DFKM_nihs1}, $\mathbf{M}$ is well-defined.
	Besides, for the operators $A$, $B$, and $C$ defined in \eqref{eq:DFKM_3op_operators}, by Lemma~\ref{le:DFKM_3op_reform}, we know that $A$ and $C$ are maximally monotone, and $B$ is monotone and $L$-Lipschitz continuous with $L_B = \max_i \{\alpha_i L_i\}$.
	Moreover, $(\mathbf{a}^{\star}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{\star}) \in \mathcal{H}^n \times \mathcal{H}^n$ satisfies
	\begin{equation*} 
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			0 \in ({A} + {B} + {C})(\mathbf{a}^{\star}, \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{\star}) \subset \mathcal{H}^n \times \mathcal{H}^n
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	if and only if $\mathbf{a}^{\star} = \Gamma^{-1}(u^{\star}, \cdots, u^{\star})$ for some $u^{\star} \in \mathbb{R}^p$ and $u^{\star}$ solves $0 \in \sum_{i=1}^n\big(G_iu^{\star} + T_iu^{\star}\big)$.
	
	Note that since the stepsizes $\alpha_i \leq \frac{1 - \tau \sigma}{2 \sqrt{1+\omega} \tau L_i}$ for all $i \in [n]$, we have $2\sqrt{1+\omega}\tau L_B + \tau \sigma \leq 1$.
	Thus, applying Theorem~\ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}, we conclude that $\{\mathbf{a}^k\}$, $\{y_{\mathbf{a}}^k\}$, $\{z_{\mathbf{a}}^k\}$, and $\{\hat{\mathbf{a}}^k\}$ converge to $\mathbf{a}^{\star} = \Gamma^{-1}(u^{\star}, \cdots, u^{\star})$.
	Since $\mathbf{x}^k = \Gamma \mathbf{a}^k$, $\hat{\mathbf{x}}^k = \Gamma \hat{\mathbf{a}}^k$, $\mathbf{t}^k = \Gamma y_{\mathbf{a}}^k$, and $\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^k = \Gamma z_{\mathbf{a}}^k$, we also get that $\{\mathbf{x}^k\}$, $\{\mathbf{t}^k\}$, $\{\mathbf{s}_{\mathbf{z}}^k\}$, and $\{\hat{\mathbf{x}}^k\}$ converge to $\mathbf{u}^{\star} = (u^{\star}, \cdots, u^{\star})$, where $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i + T_i))$.
		
	                                             
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent\textbf{(ii)~The convergence of consensus error.}
	From \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof2} of Theorem~\ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}, we know that 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_NI_convergence_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big),
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $\vartheta^k \in C^{-1}v^k$, which is equivalent to $v^k \in C\vartheta^k$.
	By the definition of $C$, we have $C\vartheta^k = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}} (\mathbf{E}\vartheta^k)$, where $\mathbf{E} = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma}, & \mathbf{M}
	\end{bmatrix}$.
	Thus, for the existence of $v^k$, we must have $\mathbf{E}\vartheta^k = 0$.
	Since $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^\top = \kappa \mathbb{I}$, we have $\Vert\mathbf{E}\Vert = \sqrt{\lambda_{\max}(\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^\top)} = \sqrt{\kappa}$.
	Then, we can derive that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_NI_convergence_proof2}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\mathbf{E}u^k\Vert = \Vert\mathbf{E}(\vartheta^k - u^k)\Vert \leq \Vert\mathbf{E}\Vert\Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert = \sqrt{\kappa}\Vert\vartheta^k - u^k\Vert.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Recall that $\mathbf{E} = \begin{bmatrix} \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma}, & \mathbf{M} \end{bmatrix}$, 
	$u^k = \begin{bmatrix} \mathbf{a}^k \\ 0 \end{bmatrix}$, 
	and $\mathbf{x}^k = \Gamma \mathbf{a}^k$, we can compute that $\mathbf{E}u^k = \mathbf{K}\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}^k = \mathbf{K}\mathbf{x}^k$.
	Combining this fact, \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_convergence_proof1}, and \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_convergence_proof2}, we get
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:DFKM_NI_convergence_proof3}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{x}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\mathbf{K}\mathbf{x}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big),
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Using these results, similarly to part (b) of Theorem~\ref{th:DFKM_convergence}, 
	we can also prove that the consensus error also converges to zero as follows:
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vert\Pi\mathbf{x}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \qquad &\text{and}& \qquad \Vert\Pi\mathbf{x}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}	
	Moreover, since $\Vert\Pi\mathbf{x}^k\Vert^2 = \Vert\mathbf{x}^k - \boldsymbol{1}\bar{x}^k\Vert^2 = \sum_{i=1}^n \Vertx_{[i]}^k - \bar{x}^k\Vert^2$, we also have
	\begin{equation*}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{rcl}	
		\Vertx_i^k - \bar{x}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad & \text{and} & \quad \Vertx_i^k - \bar{x}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big) \quad \text{for all $i \in [n]$}.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	
	                                                   
	\vspace{0.75ex}
	\noindent\textbf{(iii)~The convergence of aggregated  residuals.}
	From \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_convergence_proof2} of Theorem~\ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}, we have
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\zeta^k + Bu^k + v^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Using $Bu^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{\Gamma} G(\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}^k) \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix} = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix}$, $\zeta^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k \\ 0
	\end{bmatrix}$ for $\boldsymbol{\xi}^k \in T(\mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{a}^k) = T\mathbf{x}^k$, and $v^k = \begin{bmatrix}
		\mathbf{b}^k \\ \breve{\mathbf{b}}^k
	\end{bmatrix}$, we obtain from the last relation that
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{b}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{b}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Moreover, since $\Vert\boldsymbol{\xi}^k + G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathbf{b}^k\Vert = \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}(\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{b}^k)\Vert \leq \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}\Vert \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{b}^k\Vert = (\min_i \sqrt{\alpha_i})^{-1} \Vert\mathbf{\Gamma} \boldsymbol{\xi}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma} G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{b}^k\Vert$, we deduce from the last relation that
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\boldsymbol{\xi}^k + G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathbf{b}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\boldsymbol{\xi}^k + G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathbf{b}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Now, since $v^k \in C\vartheta^k = \mathbf{E}^\top \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(\mathbf{E}\vartheta^k)$, there exists some $\mathbf{s}^k \in \mathcal{N}_{\{0\}}(0)$ such that $v^k = \mathbf{E}^\top\mathbf{s}^k$.
	Using $\mathbf{E}^\top = \begin{bmatrix}
		\Gamma K \\ M
	\end{bmatrix}$, we get $\mathbf{b}^k = \mathbf{\Gamma} \mathbf{K} s^k$, thus $\mathbf{\Gamma}^{-1}\mathbf{b^k} = \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k$.
	Substituting this relation into the last expression, we get
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{ccc}
		\Vert\boldsymbol{\xi}^k + G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) \quad &\text{and}& \quad \Vert\boldsymbol{\xi}^k + G\mathbf{x}^k + \mathbf{K}\mathbf{s}^k\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Utilizing these results, similar to part (c) of Theorem~\ref{th:DFKM_convergence}, we can also prove that the aggregated residual converges to zero as
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{rcl}	
			\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i x_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert = \mathcal{O}\big(1/k\big) 
			\qquad &\text{and}& \qquad 
			\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n (G_i x_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k)\Vert = o\big(1/k\big).
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	   
	Finally, it is easy to prove that the average operator $\bar{G}$ is $\bar{L}$-Lipschitz continuous with $\bar{L} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n L_i$.
	Then, using the above convergence results together with Theorem \ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence}(ii), all the ingredients required in the proofs of Theorem ~\ref{th:DFKM_convergence}(iv)–(vi) are satisfied. 
	Hence, parts (iv)–(vi) follow by similar arguments, and we omit the details.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- theorem-8: body_md -->
\label{th:NI_DFKM2_convergence}
	For \eqref{eq:DGE}, suppose that $G_i$ is $\frac{1}{L}$-co-coercive and $T_i$ is maximally monotone for all $i \in [n]$. 
	Let $\{\mathbf{x}^k\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme2} using  $t_0$ given in \eqref{eq:t0_cond2} and the following parameters:
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:NI-DFKM2_params}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{c}
			r > 1, \qquad 0 < \nu < r - 1, \qquad 0 < \alpha_i < \frac{2(1 - \tau \sigma)}{\tau L_i}, \vspace{1ex}\\
			2\kappa > \Vert\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}(\mathbb{I} - W)\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}\Vert, \qquad 
			t_{k} := k +  t_0, \qquad \text{and} \qquad \nu_k := \frac{\nu(t_k - 1)}{t_k},
		\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	where $\mathbf{\Lambda} := \mathrm{diag}\left(\alpha_1, \cdots, \alpha_n\right)$.
	Then, the following statements hold.
	\begin{compactenum}
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(i)}$~\textbf{$($Local iterates$)$}]
	The local iterates $\{x_{[i]}^k\}$ converge to the same $u^\star \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$ of the original \eqref{eq:DGE} problem for all nodes $i \in [n]$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(ii)}$~\textbf{$($Consensus error$)$}]
	The consensus error $\Vert\Pi \mathbf{x}^k \Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iii)}$~\textbf{$($Aggregated residual$)$}]
	The aggregated residual $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i x_{[i]}^k + \xi_i^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\xi_i^k \in Tx_{[i]}^k$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(iv)}$~\textbf{$($Restricted gap$)$}]
	If, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(\bar{x}^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $\bar{x}^k := \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n x_{[i]}^k$.
	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\mathrm{Gap}_B(p^k)$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{x}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(v)}$~\textbf{$($FBS residual$)$}]
	For a given $\lambda \in (0, \frac{1}{\bar{L}})$ with $\bar{L} = \frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^n L_i$, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}\bar{x}^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
