	Otherwise, if, in addition, Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity} holds, then $\Vert\mathcal{G}_{\lambda}p^k\Vert^2$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$, where $p^k := \mathrm{proj}_{\mathcal{B}}(\bar{x}^k)$.
	
	\item[$~~~\mathrm{(vi)}$~\textbf{$($Special case$)$}]
	If $T_i = 0$ for all $i \in [n]$ $($i.e., \eqref{eq:DGE} reduces to \eqref{eq:DNE}$)$, then $\Vert\sum_{i=1}^n G_i \bar{x}^k\Vert$ converges to zero at the rate of $o\big(1/k\big)$.
	\end{compactenum}
<!-- end theorem-8 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
Since $0 < \alpha_i < \frac{2(1 - \tau \sigma)}{\tau L_i}$, we can show that $\frac{1}{2}\tau L_B + \tau \sigma < 1$.
	Therefore, all the conditions of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_th2_params} in Theorem~\ref{th:FKM_3OP_convergence2} are satisfied.
	The remainder of this proof is very similar to that of Theorem~\ref{th:NI_DFKM_convergence}, and hence is omitted.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
Although Algorithm~\ref{alg:NI_DFFP} requires $2\kappa > \Vert\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}(\mathbb{I} - \mathbf{W})\mathbf{\Lambda}^{1/2}\Vert$, in practice, we can choose a valid value $\kappa = \max_{ i \in [n]} \alpha_i$ which does not requires spectral information of the network.
	More importantly, the local stepsize bound $0 < \alpha_i \leq \frac{1 - \tau \sigma}{2 \sqrt{1+\omega} \tau L_i}$ or $0 < \alpha_i < \frac{2(1 - \tau \sigma)}{\tau L_i}$ depends only on $L_i$, allowing each agent to exploit its own local information instead of using a common stepsize determined by the worst local operator and network topology.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{le:A1}
	Let $\{u_k\}$, $\{v_k\}$, $\{\gamma_k\}$, and $\{\varepsilon_k\}$ be nonnegative sequences such that $\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_k < +\infty$ and $\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon_k < +\infty$.
	In addition, for all $k \geq 0$, we assume that
	\begin{equation*}
		u_{k+1} \leq (1+\gamma_k) u_k - v_k + \varepsilon_k.
	\end{equation*}
	Then, we conclude that $\lim_{k \to \infty} u_k$ exists and $\sum_{k=0}^\infty v_k < +\infty$.
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{le:A2}
Let $\{\xi_k\}$ be a nonnegative sequence and $s \in \mathbb{R}$ such that $\lim_{k \to \infty} k^{s+1} \xi_k$ exists and $\sum_{k=0}^{\infty} k^s \xi_k < +\infty$.
Then, we have $\lim_{k \to \infty} k^{s+1}\xi_k = 0$.
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{le:norm_equivalence}
	Let $P \in \mathbb{R}^{p \times p}$ be a symmetric positive definite matrix.
	Then 
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:norm_equivalence}
		\lambda_{\min}(P) \VertP^{-1}z\Vert_P^2 \leq \Vertz\Vert_2^2 \leq \lambda_{\max}(P) \VertP^{-1}z\Vert_P^2, \quad \textrm{for any $z \in \mathbb{R}^p$},
	\end{equation}
	where $\lambda_{\min}(P)$ and $\lambda_{\max}(P)$ are the smallest and largest eigenvalues of $P$, respectively.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{le:monotone_local_bounded}
	Let $T: \mathbb{R}^p \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^p$ be monotone and $\mathcal{X}$ be a compact subset in $\mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T))$. 
	Then, $T(\mathcal{X})$ is bounded.
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{le:sum_maximal_monotone}
	Let $T = T_1 + T_2$ for $T_i: \mathbb{R}^p \rightrightarrows \mathbb{R}^p$ maximally monotone for $i=1,2$.
	If $\mathrm{ri}\left(\mathrm{dom}(T_1)\right) \cap \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathrm{dom}(T_2)\right) \ne \emptyset$, then $T$ is maximally monotone.
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{le:DFKM_nihs1}
	Let $S, T: \mathbb{R}^p \to \mathbb{R}^p$ be self-adjoint operators and $\kappa \in \mathbb{R}_{++}$ be given.
	If $\VertTS^2T\Vert < \kappa$, then $\kappa\mathbb{I} - ST^2S \succ 0$.
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
Let $\mathbf{L} \in \mathbb{R}^{m \times n}$ be a matrix with $0 < \mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{L}) \leq \min\{m,n\}$.
	Then, for any $\mathbf{s} \in \mathrm{Null}(\mathbf{L})^{\perp}$, we have 
	\begin{equation*}
		\Vert\mathbf{L}\mathbf{s}\Vert \geq \sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{L})\Vert\mathbf{s}\Vert, 
	\end{equation*}		
	where $\sigma_{\min}^+(\mathbf{L})$ is the smallest positive singular value of $\mathbf{L}$.
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- head-29: body_md -->
Let $\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}$ be the normal cone of $\mathcal{B}$, then since $\mathcal{B}$ is closed and convex set, $\mathcal{N}_{\mathcal{B}}$ is maximally monotone.
	From the construction of $\mathcal{B}$ under Assumption~\ref{as:restricted_dual_gap}, we have 
	\begin{equation*}
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\emptyset \ne \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathcal{B}\right) \subseteq \mathcal{B} \subset \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{int}(\mathrm{dom}(T_i)) = \mathrm{int}\big(\bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{dom}(T_i)\big) = \mathrm{int}\big(\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)\big) \subseteq \mathrm{ri}\left(\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)\right),
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Thus, applying Lemma~\ref{le:sum_maximal_monotone}, the operator $\Psi := \Phi + \mathcal{N}_B$ is maximally monotone.
<!-- end head-29 -->
<!-- head-30: body_md -->
From the construction of $\mathcal{B}$ under Assumption~\ref{as:boundary_regularity}$\mathrm{(i)}$, we have
	\begin{equation*}
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\mathcal{B} = \bigcap_{i=1}^n \mathrm{dom}(T_i) = \mathrm{dom}(\Phi).
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Therefore, the inequality \eqref{eq:le_gap_function_proof1} holds on the entire domain $\mathrm{dom}(\Phi)$, leading to
	\begin{equation*} 
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		\langle \sum_{i=1}^n (G_i u + \xi_i) - 0, u - u^{\star}\rangle \geq 0 \qquad \forall u \in \mathrm{dom}(\Phi), \quad \xi_i \in T_i u.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	Since $\Phi$ is maximally monotone, the last inequality implies that $(u^{\star}, 0) \in \mathrm{gra}(\Phi)$.
	Thus, we have $0 \in \Phi(u^{\star})$, or equivalently, $u^{\star} \in \mathrm{zer}(\Phi)$.
\hfill $\square$
<!-- end head-30 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{le:NI_DFFP_derivation2}
	Let $\{(\hat{v}^k, z^k)\}$ be generated by \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme}.
	Then, we have $\hat{v}^k, z_v^k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$ and $\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^\top\mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k = \hat{v}^k$ for all $k \geq 0$.
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
In \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, let us denote $z_v^{k+1} = z_v^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau\sigma)}Q_{k+1}$, where $Q_{k+1} := \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_u^{k+1} + \sigma \tilde{w}_v^{k+1}$.
	From the seventh and eighth lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, we can derive that
	\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			Q_{k+1} &=& (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k (\tau \sigma \tilde{w}_u^k + \sigma \tilde{w}_v^k) = (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k Q_k.
		\end{array}
	\end{equation*}
	We will prove that $\hat{v}^k, z_v^k, Q_k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$ for all $k \geq 0$ by induction.
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item \textit{The base step}: 
		From the initialization of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, we have $z_v^0 = v^0$, and thus, $\hat{v}^0 = \frac{t_0 - r}{t_0}v^0 + \frac{r}{t_0}z_v^0 = v^0$.
		Let us choose $v^0 = \frac{\sigma}{\kappa}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\Big(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix}\Big)$, then $v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$, and thus $\hat{v}^0 = z_v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$.
		Next, let $c^0 := u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0$.
		Then, we can compute that
		\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathbf{E}c^0 = \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) - \frac{1}{\sigma}\mathbf{E}v^0
			= \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) - \frac{1}{\kappa}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) = 0.
		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Therefore, $v^0 \in Cc^0$, hence we can choose $\tilde{w}_u^0 = \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} + v^0$ and $\tilde{w}_v^0 = c^0 - u^0 = -\tau \begin{bmatrix}
		\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0$.
		Thus, we can compute that 
		\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			Q_0 &=& \tau\sigma\tilde{w}_u^0 + \sigma\tilde{w}_v^0 = \tau\sigma\left(\begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} + v^0\right) + \sigma\left(-\tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0\right) = -(1 - \tau\sigma)v^0.
		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Since $v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$, the last expression implies that $Q_0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$.
		
		\item \textit{The induction step}: 
		Since $v^{k+1} = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1} = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\left(\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1}\right)$, we have $v^{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		From the inductive hypothesis, we already have $\hat{v}^k, z_v^k, Q_k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		Thus, we conclude that $Q_{k+1} = (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k Q_k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		Using this fact, we also get $z_v^{k+1} = z_v^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau \sigma)}Q_{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$, and $\hat{v}^{k+1} = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}} v^{k+1} + \frac{r}{t_{k+1}}z_v^{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
	\end{compactitem}
	Finally, since $\hat{v}^k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$, there exists $\xi^k$ such that $\hat{v}^k = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k$.
	Then, using the fact that $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top} = \kappa\mathbb{I}$, we have $\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}(\kappa\mathbb{I})\xi^k = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k = \hat{v}^k$.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- head-31: body_md -->
From the initialization of \eqref{eq:FKM_scheme}, we have $z_v^0 = v^0$, and thus, $\hat{v}^0 = \frac{t_0 - r}{t_0}v^0 + \frac{r}{t_0}z_v^0 = v^0$.
		Let us choose $v^0 = \frac{\sigma}{\kappa}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\Big(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix}\Big)$, then $v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$, and thus $\hat{v}^0 = z_v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$.
		Next, let $c^0 := u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0$.
		Then, we can compute that
		\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			\mathbf{E}c^0 = \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) - \frac{1}{\sigma}\mathbf{E}v^0
			= \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) - \frac{1}{\kappa}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^\top \mathbf{E}\left(u^0 - \tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}\right) = 0.
		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Therefore, $v^0 \in Cc^0$, hence we can choose $\tilde{w}_u^0 = \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} + v^0$ and $\tilde{w}_v^0 = c^0 - u^0 = -\tau \begin{bmatrix}
		\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
		\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0$.
		Thus, we can compute that 
		\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			Q_0 &=& \tau\sigma\tilde{w}_u^0 + \sigma\tilde{w}_v^0 = \tau\sigma\left(\begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} + v^0\right) + \sigma\left(-\tau \begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0\right) = -(1 - \tau\sigma)v^0.
		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Since $v^0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$, the last expression implies that $Q_0 \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^\top)$.
<!-- end head-31 -->
<!-- head-32: body_md -->
Since $v^{k+1} = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1} = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\left(\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}\tilde{v}^{k+1}\right)$, we have $v^{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		From the inductive hypothesis, we already have $\hat{v}^k, z_v^k, Q_k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		Thus, we conclude that $Q_{k+1} = (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{v}^k - v^{k+1}) + \beta_k Q_k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
		Using this fact, we also get $z_v^{k+1} = z_v^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau \sigma)}Q_{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$, and $\hat{v}^{k+1} = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}} v^{k+1} + \frac{r}{t_{k+1}}z_v^{k+1} \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$.
	\end{compactitem}
	Finally, since $\hat{v}^k \in \mathrm{Im}(\mathbf{E}^{\top})$, there exists $\xi^k$ such that $\hat{v}^k = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k$.
	Then, using the fact that $\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top} = \kappa\mathbb{I}$, we have $\kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\hat{v}^k = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k = \kappa^{-1}\mathbf{E}^{\top}(\kappa\mathbb{I})\xi^k = \mathbf{E}^{\top}\xi^k = \hat{v}^k$.
<!-- end head-32 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{le:NI_DFFP_derivation3}
	In the scheme \eqref{eq:DFKM_NI_scheme_init}, we always have $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k = 0$, $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, and $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, for all $k \geq 0$.
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Denote by $z_u^{k+1} = z_u^k - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau\sigma)}R_{k+1}$ from the scheme \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, where $R_{k+1} := \tau \tilde{w}_u^{k+1} + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_v^{k+1}$.
	From  the seventh and eighth lines of \eqref{eq:FKM_3OP_scheme}, we can show that
	\begin{equation}\label{eq:NI_DFFP_derivation3_proof1}
	\arraycolsep=0.2em
	\begin{array}{lcl}
		R_{k+1}  &=& (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{u}^k - u^{k+1}) + \beta_k (\tau \tilde{w}_u^k + \tau \sigma \tilde{w}_v^k) 
		= (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{u}^k - u^{k+1}) + \beta_k R_k.
	\end{array}
	\end{equation}
	Let $u^k = (\mathbf{a}^k, \breve{\mathbf{a}}^k)$  and other notations be  as in Subsection~\ref{subsec:NI_DFFP_derivation}.
	Let $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_{k+1}$ be the second (fictitious) block of $R_k$ corresponding to $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k$.
	We will prove by induction that $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k = 0$, $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, and $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_k = 0$ for all $k \geq 0$.
	Indeed, we proceed as follows.
	\begin{compactitem}
		\item \textit{The base step:} 
		Since we initialize $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^0 = 0$ and $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^0 = 0$, we have $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^0 = \frac{t_0 - r}{t_0}\breve{\mathbf{a}}^0 + \frac{r}{t_0}z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^0 = 0$.
		Moreover, from the base step of Lemma~\ref{le:NI_DFFP_derivation2}, we can compute that
		\begin{equation*}
		\arraycolsep=0.2em
		\begin{array}{lcl}
			R_0 &=& \tau\tilde{w}_u^0 + \tau\sigma\tilde{w}_v^0 = \tau\left(\begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} + v^0\right) + \tau\sigma\left(-\tau \begin{bmatrix}
				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix} - \frac{1}{\sigma}v^0\right) = \tau(1 - \tau\sigma)\begin{bmatrix}
			\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0
			\end{bmatrix}.
		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Thus, the second block $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_0 = 0$.
		  
		\item \textit{The induction step:} 
		Assume that for $k \geq 0$, we have $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k = 0$, $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, and $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_k = 0$.
		From \textbf{Step 3} of Subsection~\ref{subsec:NI_DFFP_derivation}, we already know that $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} = 0$.
		Next, we can compute the fictitious block of \eqref{eq:NI_DFFP_derivation3_proof1} as $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_{k+1} = (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_k = 0$.
		Moreover, we also get $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^a - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau\sigma)}\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_{k+1} = 0$ and $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}}\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} + \frac{r}{t_{k+1}}z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = 0$.
	\end{compactitem}
	By induction, we complete the proof.
<!-- end proof-13 -->

				\boldsymbol{\Gamma}\mathbf{h}^0 \\ 0

		\end{array}
		\end{equation*}
		Thus, the second block $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$}

<!-- head-34: body_md -->
Assume that for $k \geq 0$, we have $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^k = 0$, $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k = 0$, and $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_k = 0$.
		From \textbf{Step 3} of Subsection~\ref{subsec:NI_DFFP_derivation}, we already know that $\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} = 0$.
		Next, we can compute the fictitious block of \eqref{eq:NI_DFFP_derivation3_proof1} as $\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_{k+1} = (1 - \tau \sigma)(\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^k - \breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1}) + \beta_k \mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_k = 0$.
		Moreover, we also get $z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^a - \frac{\nu_k}{r(1 - \tau\sigma)}\mathpalette\let\was@math@style=\setbox124=\hbox{$\was@math@styleR$} 
\setbox125=\hbox{$\fam=3\global\knuthian@fdfive=\fontdimen5\font$}
\setbox125=\hbox{$\widetilde{\vrule height 0pt depth 0pt width \wd124}$} 
               \baselineskip=1pt\relax
               \vtop{\copy124\copy125\vskip -\knuthian@fdfive}\relax_{k+1} = 0$ and $\hat{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = \frac{t_{k+1} - r}{t_{k+1}}\breve{\mathbf{a}}^{k+1} + \frac{r}{t_{k+1}}z_{\breve{\mathbf{a}}}^{k+1} = 0$.
	\end{compactitem}
	By induction, we complete the proof.
